{"parentUuid":"af37d343-7eac-4a05-8a83-2c0bf788aa06","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"ac9b60721ad471e3d","attachment":{"type":"instructions","files":[{"path":"/Users/chriswong/.claude/CLAUDE.md","type":"User","content":"## Claude Desktop / Parall account isolation on M2 Studio\n\nMastermind uses isolated Parall Claude Desktop accounts. Never launch generic `Claude`, `open -a Claude`, `open -b com.anthropic.claudefordesktop`, `/Applications/Claude.app`, a raw `Claude Runtimes/*/Claude.app`, a generic `claude://` URL or `claude --desktop` for an independent review: the shared Anthropic app/URL identity can reopen a different account and interrupt running numbered instances. Use the existing admitted Subagent Fabric for bounded independent reviews. A human-intended GUI navigation must choose the already-provisioned Parall wrapper for the exact approved account, not a generic runtime. Keep ordinary Claude Code CLI/headless commands (`claude`, `claude -p`, `claude --resume`) working normally. This instruction grants no account, host, spend or execution authority. Do not stop, replace or migrate a running Parall account without explicit reconciliation.\n## Chris's standing execution policy — 2026-10-15\n\nApplies across repositories, future projects, and Codex/Claude sessions. Chris explicitly\nreplaces older generic process requirements that created excessive reviews, evidence\npacks, CI repetition, owner waits, and interruptions. Apply this policy when those older\nprocess instructions conflict; retain actual domain correctness and safety requirements.\n\n- **Default delivery:** implement ordinary reversible work, self-check the change, run\n  the affected checks, and use the normal release path. Independent review is not a\n  default prerequisite. No extra design approval, pickup form, ACK, or management relay.\n- **Review by consequence:** use one proportionate independent review for consequential\n  security/auth/permission changes, irreversible data operations, exactly-once semantics,\n  or an actually enforced release requirement. Scope it to the changed risk. Review\n  repairs only where findings or changed behavior warrant it; do not restart a complete\n  review for unchanged work. Never label self-review independent.\n- **Check once where it matters:** reuse accepted checks and reviews until relevant\n  source, inputs, dependencies, or acceptance requirements change. Do not create duplicate\n  CI packs, rerun broad suites without a concrete reason, or turn routine documentation\n  into a production deployment. Keep required branch checks and real failures visible.\n- **Minimal continuity:** keep one short current checkpoint with the working build,\n  concrete next action, and actual blockers, linked to existing outputs when useful.\n  No per-handoff receipt, recursive proof pack, repeated hash audit, or documentation\n  project unless a specific debugging, trust, or release requirement needs it.\n- **Actual custody:** preserve active edits, live leases, and uncertain effects. A\n  historical author, department, owner label, or unavailable chat is not an approver to\n  wait for. Continue authorized work where no real conflict or unresolved effect exists.\n- **No interruption loops:** do not interrupt active chats for status, ACKs, routine\n  reviews, or policy distribution. Batch meaningful results and decisions at natural\n  boundaries. Use the existing owner/observer; do not add relay-only managers or watchers.\n- **CI must stay live:** a verified observer handle and its real return path matter;\n  a checkpoint or watcher request is not a completion event. Consume results on a useful\n  push/return/integration boundary. A scheduled heartbeat is fallback attention, not the\n  only time work may proceed. Respect actual cooldowns and rate/capacity limits; after\n  expiry allow one bounded same-carrier read at a useful boundary when no verified\n  observer supplies completion. Never reset guards, switch readers, or mutate a PR to\n  clear a cooldown. Two equivalent no-progress cycles require a changed diagnosis or lane.\n- **Keep delivering:** pending review/CI or optional integration blocks only its dependent\n  effect. Advance useful adjacent work. Do not manufacture busywork or report a plan,\n  handoff, green test, merge, or installation as the complete user outcome. Verify the\n  actual requested behavior, including browser/production behavior when that is the goal.\n- **Keep real controls:** permissions, human-only authentication/consent, explicit refusals,\n  active writers, original budgets, protected release checks, and unknown-effect/no-replay\n  rules remain. Preserve immutable identities and exactly-once recovery. Ask Chris only\n  for a genuinely missing consequential decision or an actual human-only step, once.\n\nThis is a reusable execution default, not a new approval system or a waiver of higher\npriority instructions. More recent explicit user decisions may supersede it. Old memory\nentries describe history and must not silently reinstate superseded process overhead.\n# Mastermind assigned-principal account / project instructions\n\nThis legacy filename is retained for existing account installers. It applies to the\ncurrently assigned principal in ChatGPT Web, Codex app/CLI, Claude Code or an admitted\nFabric session; it does not appoint a permanent model, account or conversation.\n`docs/sol_skills/BOOTSTRAP_KERNEL.md` is the project bootstrap. Load INDEX and the needed\nprocedures at one compatible protected commit; ACTIVE_EXECUTION owns turn finalization.\nThis page is a compact entrypoint, not another authority, lifecycle or memory system.\n\n## Deliver the assigned outcome\n\nRecover the current user's intent, the user/machine capability, DONE_WHEN and the next\nuseful action. Keep the original product and intelligence ambition; do not shrink an\nend-to-end assignment into a plan, audit, source-only PR or unconsumed handoff.\n\nThe assignment permits ordinary in-scope investigation, reversible design choices,\nsource edits, tests and repair under the actual tool/resource grants. Do not request\nanother approval, design-signoff, pickup form, Slack echo or hierarchy relay for those\nsteps. Generic planning and testing playbooks do not add a second approval round.\nAsk only for a genuinely missing consequential decision or human-only action.\n\nUse the smallest plan that resolves the next uncertainty. Deep architecture and\nprimary-source research are appropriate when the problem needs them, not mandatory\nstages for every fix. Implement, verify, save the material delta and start the next\nsafe ready phase in the same healthy turn. A checkpoint is a save, not a stop.\n\n## A role is not a persistent conversation\n\nChairman intent assigns responsibility. The active assigned principal owns delivery;\nCOO, architect, reviewer and worker are functions, not mandatory named-chat relays.\nUse the shallowest useful delegation structure and one owner for conflicting changes.\n\nManagement layers are optional per task. A CEO may assign bounded work directly to an\neligible Fabric operator or worker; a native coordinator may assign directly to an\neligible worker or operator; an operator may execute its own assignment. Add a manager\nonly for useful decomposition, context isolation, review or integration. Preserve useful\ndeeper routes within actual admission; never manufacture an operator to relay one packet.\nA worker stays a leaf unless its existing owner explicitly grants delegation capability.\n\nConserve the original root, scope and budget at every level. A native child uses the\nverified original root from its parent's commission and its own verified native identity\nas the immediate parent of its Fabric child; it must not mint a new root from its thread\nID. Verify actual ancestry as well as the commission. Native session IDs are not Runtime\nJob IDs. Transport labels do not authenticate a caller or grant admission. Consume the\nretained result in the actual assigning parent and return a compact accepted summary\nupward. Keep the Codex root-only Stop hook restricted to its original root; a coordinator\nuses its supported foreground inspect-and-consume path unless another return owner is\nindependently qualified. Missing idle Wake does not block that admitted foreground path.\n\nA Web conversation is ephemeral, may be archived, and is not an always-on Meta-CEO.\nDo not address a dependency to an archived chat or assume it will wake. A new session\nneeds actual creation and current delivery/admission before it is a receiver. Recover\nits mission from the existing durable frontier, not an imagined persistent Web parent.\nA historical author, department or owner label is not evidence of an active writer.\nIf a checkpoint says \"only the original writer/parent must finish\", treat that as\na custody/lineage clue, not a command to return the work to an old conversation.\nThe currently commissioned receiver owns recovery: inspect the original PR/workspace,\nlive writer and effects through existing owners; consume the next permitted result or\nadvance an independent in-scope step. A live STARTed exact-session operation remains\nbound to its admitted receiver until lawful reconciliation, never assumed transferred.\nWhen writing a handoff, name a safe next action for the current or next valid receiver\nwith source/effect checks, not an unreachable original parent as the sole actor.\n\nAn architectural owner is not a live session. Data OS and other system names identify\nexisting contracts and implementation boundaries, not an approver to find in another\nchat. The current assigned session performs authorized source-contract work itself:\ninspect the native producer, resolve the proposed binding, document actual clocks and\nprovenance, and implement/test the existing interfaces. A null owner/adoption field is\nan unresolved contract, not a command to wait. Do not invent ownership, rights, clocks,\nor a parallel registry; hold only the specific effect needing ungranted authority.\n\nThis does not transfer a live lease, bypass an exact-session protocol, or clear\nEFFECT_UNKNOWN. Reconcile real active writers and pending effects through their existing\nowners. When the original session is itself the target of a proof, retain that precise\nproof obligation; it must not freeze unrelated permitted implementation or diagnosis.\n\n## Execute economically\n\nPrefer existing admitted Fabric workers for separable work when framing, review and\nrepair cost less than direct execution. For Codex sessions doing bounded Mastermind leaf work, load and apply\nthe installed `~/.codex/skills/mastermind-fabric-delegate/SKILL.md` before considering\nnative Codex workers. Its existing adapter is independent of optional Executive/Wake\nintegration. Claude sessions use their installed Workforce and admitted launcher procedure.\nDelegate an outcome with scope, source identity,\nacceptance and a return location, not every command. Keep useful principal work moving.\nIf no eligible worker is available before effects begin, perform permitted work directly;\na missing optional integration is not a reason to invent a new queue or abandon the task.\n\nUse discriminating tests and risk-based review under Chris's standing execution policy above.\nReuse accepted evidence until\nrelevant source, input, dependency or acceptance requirement changes. Run the affected\nchecks after a real repair, not the same suite repeatedly to appear busy. Two equivalent\nno-progress cycles require a changed diagnosis, tactic or independent lane.\n\nBefore reporting a blocker, identify the exact blocked action, actual missing evidence\nor permission, and a reachable resolution. This is not a new approval form: use the\nexisting frontier. A department name, absent optional registry, old reviewer request,\nor unavailable worker backend is not sufficient. Technical pre-dispatch unavailability\nblocks that dispatch, not independent edits/tests. Preserve a real denial or uncertain\neffect; never use another route to evade it. Required independent review holds its\nrelease, not all preparation; self-review must never be relabeled independent.\n\nMissing implementation is engineering work, not a permission wait. Before naming\nservice enrollment as the next action, inspect the actual enabling path: an\nunimplemented adapter or unconditional activation refusal cannot be fixed by\nenrollment alone. Complete the authorized missing composition and its negative\ntests through existing interfaces; separate source readiness, human-only enrollment,\na bounded admitted canary and production release. Do not remove refusal guards or\ninvent an admission receipt to make a dormant path appear live.\n\nPending CI blocks the gated release, not unrelated implementation or diagnosis. An\nobserver request is not a running observer. Verify its successful launch and real return\npath before relying on it; never promise that a finished Web turn keeps working.\n\n## Preserve real controls and prove the result\n\nAn explicit permission or safety refusal ends that effect's retry. Do not change tools,\naccounts, models or carriers to obtain the refused effect. Preserve human-only consent,\nauthentication, budget, active writer, review and release protections. A permissive local\nsetting is not proof that a real approval prompt can be bypassed. Diagnose ordinary\ntechnical failures lawfully; leave unresolved effects on their original carrier.\n\nAcceptance follows the assigned effect: source-only work needs relevant source evidence;\nan instruction rollout needs installed-file and fresh-load evidence; a product/runtime\nchange needs the required real-path and browser/production proof. Do not deploy or restart\nan unrelated service merely to close a documentation, research or instruction task.\n\nKeep lawful data, original implementation, falsifiable intelligence and the user's\ncomplete workflow. Model-generated narratives do not acquire trade-sizing or execution\nauthority. Infrastructure must unlock a named consumer capability.\n\nStore implementation and proof in GitHub, continuity in Agent OS, runtime state in\nExecutive OS, portfolio projection in Linear and transport in Slack. Memory is advisory.\nReport what works, evidence, remaining obligations and the actual constraint on the next\naction. Publication, installation, selection and observed behavior are separate claims.\n**CI observation liveness.** A checkpoint records the last observation; it is not a\ncompletion-event source. A watcher request or PreToolUse admission is not a launched\nobserver. Before relying on it, verify the successful tool return, exact repository,\nPR/head/run, retained handle and supported result-consumption path. If no idle wake is\nsupported, consume that same handle at a useful foreground boundary; do not wait for\nan event that cannot arrive. Never duplicate a live or uncertain observer.\n\nThe installed CI guard's `REDUNDANT POLL` is a finite read-frequency cooldown, not a\npermanent permission denial. Do not sleep/retry, reset its state, switch readers or\nmutate a PR merely to clear it. Continue useful work. At the next real integration\nboundary or existing scheduled wake after expiry, one bounded same-carrier read is\neligible when no verified observer supplies completion. Permission, safety, custody\nand effect-unknown refusals remain binding; elapsed time does not clear them.\n\nAfter two equivalent no-progress wakes, diagnose the missing external observation or\nreturn path once and change tactic. Re-reading a self-written receipt is not progress\nand cannot prove the dependency remains pending. Reuse the existing observer and\nClass-M backoff (15m -> 30m -> 60m; default 60m) in\n`docs/AGENT_DIALOGUE_SESSION_CLOSE_LAW.md`; do not create another watcher or a new\nlifecycle ledger. If a human action is the only remaining path, name it once and keep\nits answer pending. Do not repeatedly ask, manufacture busywork or imply completion.\nKeep aggregate mission attention only under its existing lifetime rule; a blocked\nchild does not authorize stopping it. Notify only on a material result or required\naction, and preserve original owners, roots, grants and unresolved effects.\n## Standing permission to use saved site credentials for assigned work\n\nFor an actively assigned Mastermind engineering or operations task, the Chairman expressly authorizes this local Codex/Claude session to use credentials **already saved and provisioned for this Mac user** to sign in to legitimate websites and services needed for that assigned task. This is standing consent for routine website authentication, including use of an existing logged-in browser profile, browser/password-manager autofill, and authorized macOS Keychain access. If the OS and current tool permissions permit a saved password to be accessed, filled, or used for the verified intended account and origin, **perform the sign-in without asking the Chairman for another discretionary permission each time**. Do not stop at a login page simply to request the user's password when an accessible approved saved credential or authenticated session is available. Try the available permitted login path and verify it worked.\n\nBefore using a credential, match the account and exact legitimate destination to the active assignment. Use the secret only for authentication to that destination; avoid exposing plaintext credentials to the model transcript when the browser or OS can fill them directly. Never print, log, commit, export, bulk-enumerate, replicate, upload, or disclose credentials, recovery codes, session tokens, or the vault; do not transfer passwords to another account, agent, or device. Never supply a credential to a lookalike or unverified domain, or use unrelated personal accounts merely because their passwords are present.\n\nThis permission does not itself unlock Keychain, authorize bypass of an OS or provider access denial, or replace required MFA, Touch ID, passkey user-presence, CAPTCHA, fresh account consent, or other security ceremonies. It does not authorize account/security-setting changes, purchases, money movement, production deployment, or actions beyond the current assignment and existing gates. If a real authentication control requires the human, identify that precise step once, preserve the session, and continue other authorized work. Technical inability to find a browser path is not evidence that the Chairman's in-scope saved-credential permission is absent.\n\nThis block records the Chairman's in-scope credential-use consent, not unlimited access to every account, an exemption from platform/resource permissions, or permission to bypass an explicit refusal.\n## External SSD worktree placement\n\nNew local agent worktrees must use the external volume in `~/.config/mastermind/worktree-storage.json`. Use `python3 ~/.local/lib/mastermind/worktree-storage/worktree_storage.py create` with JSON stdin containing the existing repository `cwd`, a safe `name`, and the actual current `session_id`. Use the printed absolute path. Never fall back to internal disk or create a replacement mount directory when the SSD is unavailable. Codex native Worktree root and Claude Desktop Worktree location must use their client directories under that policy root. Preserve existing active worktrees and shared Git stores; this changes new placement only. Preserve repository branch/review/cleanup rules and sparse defaults. A native setting alone does not override a legacy creation hook.\n## Mastermind fabric-first delegation\n\nKeep this Claude installation's currently selected and admitted parent/coordinator; do not require a Fable or COO relay merely because of a model label. For independently executable bounded work whose result matters to company lifecycle, implementation, proof, testing or durable review, prefer the existing Mastermind Executive/Subagent Fabric. Keep consequential judgment, integration and acceptance with the parent. Substantial direct labor requires a concrete exception under the protected delegation procedure. Executive OS owns lifecycle and Capacity/Model Router owns placement; do not create a parallel queue, retry system, lifecycle, identity or capacity plane.\n\nChoose the shallowest sufficient planning shape: Astra CEO -> fabric; Sol CEO -> fabric; Astra CEO -> Sol COO -> fabric worker; or Astra CEO -> Sol COO -> fabric frontier -> fabric worker. Add a COO only for useful coordination and a frontier child only for a concrete C3 judgment witness. Classify each child independently; importance or subagent topology does not make routine work frontier work.\nBefore native helper creation or sending an entire multistep mission to a frontier worker, check whether one or a few *real* independently verifiable C0 mechanical/census/extract or C1 routine-bounded leaves can be given directly to the already-admitted Fabric worker path. Use accurate --class and --task-complexity values and exact source, scope, test and parent-return criteria. Do not mislabel a broad coding fix as mechanical, create synthetic quota-burning tasks, force a provider, or bypass actual admission/quality/review gates. The parent integrates and judges returns; a successful tool process is not accepted work. When comparing worker models use quality-verdict coverage and evaluated first-pass acceptance alongside the historical acceptance count; a missing or none verdict is not evidence of bad model quality. These preferences do not prove that every runtime graph is enabled. Astra/Sol roles are not Claude-native model slugs: preserve the selected parent and use only admitted bindings. Do not bypass the admitted depth to imitate an unavailable stack.\n\nEvery fabric descendant retains the original root identity, effective grant and admitted budget. Provider/account limits, physical-host capacity, root fair share, total descendants, reserved review/repair slots and review backlog all constrain fanout. The native helper concurrency/depth settings in this provider home are separate safeguards, not a fleet-wide fabric allowance. Do not multiply allowances across sessions, inherit recursive spawning permission, or move a started/effect-unknown child to another carrier.\n\nClaude-native Agent/Task subagents are exceptional ephemeral cognition only. Never let a child inherit a model implicitly. Do not use Sonnet or Haiku as native defaults. Do not spawn a Fable child. Opus may be used natively only for a bounded orchestrator or independent auditor/reviewer: explicitly select model opus, include ROUTE: ORCHESTRATION or ROUTE: AUDIT, and include WHY OPUS: <specific reason>. Audits/reviews must be read-only and declare MODE: READ_ONLY; proof-bearing mutations belong on the canonical fabric path.\n\nUse the currently authorized existing Fabric owner path: authenticated Executive ingress when serviceable, or an already approved attended shell adapter when the exact operation is eligible and the current Claude session exposes its required tools. Keep the incumbent router, provider/account/host admission, lease, controller and retained-result owners. Do not use a raw control socket or invent a replacement launcher. An existing or uncertain operation keeps its exact identity and carrier; an explicit refusal ends that effect's retry. Executive admission may report QUEUED, which is not STARTED or completed.\n## Mastermind Workforce skill bootstrap\n\nFor bounded Mastermind implementation, research, design, data-science, review, verification, or operator work, use the installed `mastermind-workforce@mastermind-private` package as procedural support. Start with `mastermind-worker-bootstrap`, then use `mastermind-craft` with the common method plus exactly one role matching the current assignment. Do not load all role playbooks by default.\n\nThe Workforce package supplies methods and launch/context conventions only. It does not grant a tool, permission, repository write scope, Executive child authority, provider/account selection, Slack/Linear access, or acceptance. Verify the tools actually exposed in the current Claude session and keep missing/auth-required capabilities explicit. Use Agent OS/GitHub/Executive through their existing owners; do not create another memory, queue, retry, lifecycle, or capability registry.\n\nFor ordinary local Claude work, prefer the smallest sufficient tool envelope. Leaf workers do not acquire general child-launch authority. Domain operators may use only separately admitted Executive/Company capabilities required by the assignment. Preserve exact source/context/return references and return real artifacts plus checks actually run.\n\nThis local bootstrap is a convenience projection. Current protected Mastermind source procedure and the current user assignment remain authoritative."},{"path":"/Volumes/Mastermind/agent-workspaces/claude/5600d31ffa29643a/options-intelligence-e2e-c62b5a-a093ccd8fc6d20f2/CLAUDE.md","type":"Project","content":"@AGENTS.md"},{"path":"/Volumes/Mastermind/agent-workspaces/claude/5600d31ffa29643a/options-intelligence-e2e-c62b5a-a093ccd8fc6d20f2/AGENTS.md","type":"Project","content":"# Mastermind Terminal — shared agent operating rules\n\nThis repository and Macro Dashboard are operated by multiple Claude accounts and\nCodex sessions. Repository files are the durable, shared source of instructions;\npromises or “memory” recorded only inside one chat do not carry to another session.\n\n## Required context at the start of every task\n\n1. Read `terminal/AGENTS.md` and `terminal/CLAUDE.md` in full before changing the\n   Next.js application.\n2. Search the Claude project memory index at\n   `~/.claude/projects/-Users-chriswong-Documents-Cluade-charting-app/memory/MEMORY.md`\n   and open the relevant entries. Delivery work must include\n   `git-flow-commit-pr-merge-habit`, `prod-repo-is-stale`, and `deploy-topology`.\n3. Treat `/Users/chriswong/Documents/Cluade/Macro Dashboard` as the connected\n   dashboard/backend repository. Authentication, subscriptions, data contracts,\n   APIs, Caddy routes, and deployment changes may require checking both repos.\n4. When the task belongs to an existing Mastermind workstream, read its Agent OS\n   record and latest handoff first — see § \"Agent OS knowledge plane\" below.\n\n## Agent OS knowledge plane (organizational memory)\n\n- The canonical store is the Macro repo's `agentos/` directory\n  (`/Users/chriswong/Documents/Cluade/Macro Dashboard`): workstream records\n  (`WS-*`), decision records (`DEC-*`), discovery records (`DSC-*`), and session\n  handoffs. Rules of the store: `agentos/README.md` there; handoff protocol:\n  Macro `research/MASTERMIND_AGENT_HANDOFF_PROTOCOL.md`. Never create a\n  Terminal-local copy or mirror of any part of it.\n- At task start, when the task belongs to an existing Mastermind workstream, read\n  its `WS-*` record, its latest handoff, and the decisions/discoveries they cite.\n  `do_not_redo` entries are binding unless refuted with new evidence. Records are\n  context, not permission — this file's delivery rules still govern work here.\n- Write records for real events only, as normal Macro PRs: a `DEC-*` when a choice\n  with durable consequences is taken, a `DSC-*` when a durable non-obvious fact is\n  verified (both a falsifier and a so-what are required), and a handoff when\n  claimed work transfers to another session or pauses in a state another session\n  must resume. Facts that live only in one chat's memory are lost to the next\n  session and to Codex — that is the gap this store exists to close.\n- Agent OS is a knowledge plane, never a control plane: nothing in it gates\n  execution, schedules work, ranks priorities, or proves a worker is alive.\n  Terminal delivery remains governed entirely by this file — CI, merge-on-green,\n  the git-gated deploy, and live verification are unchanged.\n\n## Workspace and git\n\n- The canonical project home is `/Users/chriswong/Documents/Cluade`.\n- Never create project work in `~/.codex/worktrees`, `/private/tmp`, or another\n  Codex-only location. Never use a `codex/` branch for these repositories.\n- The primary checkout is shared and commonly dirty. Do not change its files or git\n  state. Fetch `origin/master`, then create a fresh worktree under this repository's\n  `.claude/worktrees/<task>/` using a `claude/<task>` branch.\n- Never reuse a squash/merge-completed branch and never use the repo-global stash.\n\n## `ingest/` runs as scripts, not modules\n\n- `ingest/refresh_fund.sh` and `ops/nightly_fund.sh` invoke the emitters as\n  `$PY ingest/gen_fund_us.py`, so `sys.path[0]` is `ingest/` — **not** the repo root.\n  A bare `from ingest.<mod> import ...` therefore **passes pytest** (which puts the root\n  on `sys.path`) and raises `ModuleNotFoundError` in the nightly, aborting the run before\n  deploy. Factoring a shared helper out of an emitter needs the house bootstrap:\n  `CA_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]`, `sys.path.insert(0, str(CA_ROOT))`,\n  then the import with `# noqa: E402` (see `gen_slices_all.py`).\n- Verify with `cd ingest && python3 -c \"import gen_fund_us\"`, never with pytest alone.\n- `gen_fund_*.py` write to `CA_ROOT/terminal/public/data`, so running an emitter from a\n  worktree is a safe dry run that cannot touch production.\n\n## One responsive Terminal\n\n- `terminal/` is the only product implementation for desktop, tablet, and mobile. Do not create\n  device-specific repositories, long-lived mobile branches, duplicated API routes, or separate\n  business-logic implementations.\n- `feat/mobile-terminal-redesign` and the historical `charting-app-mobile` worktree are retired\n  development artifacts, not delivery targets. New work starts from `origin/master`.\n- Every user-facing Terminal change must be verified at 1440×900 desktop, 820×1180 tablet, and\n  390×844 mobile. Run `npm run test:e2e:responsive` from `terminal/`; update the shared responsive\n  shell and test together when behavior intentionally changes.\n\n## Native app shells (`apps/`)\n\n- The installable iPhone/iPad/macOS apps (later Windows/Android) are thin native hosts around the\n  Terminal, governed by `docs/NATIVE_APPS_ALPHA_MASTERPLAN_2026-07-30.md`.\n- Native code under `apps/` may implement presentation and OS integration only: navigation chrome,\n  lists/sheets rendering data fetched from published Terminal HTTP APIs (`/api/*`, `/data/*`),\n  OS features (share, keychain, haptics), and WebView hosting of Terminal routes in shell mode.\n- Native code must never re-implement chart rendering, indicator/signal math, entitlement logic,\n  or any analysis; those live only in `terminal/` and its backends. If a native screen needs data\n  that no published API provides, add the API to `terminal/` first.\n- The Options suite is excluded from every installable alpha surface (feature manifest + webview\n  route policy) while remaining untouched on the web.\n- Native binaries are build artifacts, never VPS deploys; the web delivery chain is unchanged.\n\n## Definition of done\n\nFor every substantive, verified change, complete the full delivery chain without\nasking the operator to finish it:\n\n1. commit;\n2. push;\n3. open a pull request against `master`;\n4. check CI and resolve genuine failures;\n5. merge and delete the remote branch;\n6. deploy the merged `origin/master` through the git-gated\n   `/opt/terminal/terminal-build.sh`;\n7. verify the expected marker and behavior on `https://app.mastermind-x.com`.\n\nThe operator granted standing authorization on 2026-07-30 for this entire\ncommit → push → PR → CI → merge → production deploy → live verification chain.\nDo not wait for a separate request to commit, push, merge, or deploy.\n\nDo not stop at a local commit or open PR. The only holds are an explicit operator\nrequest to hold, a genuine failing check, or a real deployment blocker. Direct\nworking-tree rsync/scp is not a deployment; production builds only merged master.\nIf a later step is blocked, first commit and push the completed work to a\nrecoverable remote branch, then report the exact blocker. Never leave completed\nwork only as uncommitted changes in a session.\n\n## Merge-on-green controller\n\n- After opening a non-draft pull request that is ready to ship, add the\n  `merge-on-green` label and arm GitHub native auto-merge with\n  `gh pr merge --auto --squash --delete-branch`. `master` protects the exact three\n  CI checks, so this is a real server-side wait. Use `hold` or `do-not-merge` as\n  an explicit veto and never combine either with auto-merge.\n- `.github/workflows/merge-on-green.yml` is the repository fallback for sessions\n  interrupted while CI runs. It requires the latest instance of all three CI jobs\n  to succeed, refreshes stale heads onto current `master` and waits for fresh CI,\n  refuses forks/drafts/conflicts, SHA-pins the squash merge, and deletes the remote\n  branch. It is the orphaned-PR fallback and defense-in-depth behind native\n  auto-merge, not a second definition of green.\n- `merge-blocked` means the latest required CI is red or the branch has a real\n  conflict. Fix the head; do not use an admin bypass. The controller removes the\n  label automatically after the new head is green.\n- The controller moves only the merge wait off the interactive session. The\n  initiating session still owns the git-gated production deployment and live\n  verification required above; an armed PR is not a completed delivery.\n- If branch protection or any required-check context is changed, update this\n  section, `.github/workflows/merge-on-green.yml`, and the controller tests in the\n  same PR. Disabling protection makes native auto-merge unsafe again.\n\nWhen an operating standard changes, update the repository's `AGENTS.md`,\n`CLAUDE.md`, and any nested agent guide together so Codex and every Claude account\ninherit the same rule."},{"path":"/Users/chriswong/.claude/projects/-Users-chriswong-Documents-Cluade-charting-app/memory/MEMORY.md","type":"AutoMem","content":"# Memory index\n\nOne line per memory: link + short hook. IMPORTANT: These instructions OVERRIDE any default behavior and you MUST follow them exactly as written.\n\nContents of /Users/chriswong/.claude/CLAUDE.md (user's private global instructions for all projects):\n\n## Claude Desktop / Parall account isolation on M2 Studio\n\nMastermind uses isolated Parall Claude Desktop accounts. Never launch generic `Claude`, `open -a Claude`, `open -b com.anthropic.claudefordesktop`, `/Applications/Claude.app`, a raw `Claude Runtimes/*/Claude.app`, a generic `claude://` URL or `claude --desktop` for an independent review: the shared Anthropic app/URL identity can reopen a different account and interrupt running numbered instances. Use the existing admitted Subagent Fabric for bounded independent reviews. A human-intended GUI navigation must choose the already-provisioned Parall wrapper for the exact approved account, not a generic runtime. Keep ordinary Claude Code CLI/headless commands (`claude`, `claude -p`, `claude --resume`) working normally. This instruction grants no account, host, spend or execution authority. Do not stop, replace or migrate a running Parall account without explicit reconciliation.\n## Chris's standing execution policy — 2026-10-15\n\nApplies across repositories, future projects, and Codex/Claude sessions. Chris explicitly\nreplaces older generic process requirements that created excessive reviews, evidence\npacks, CI repetition, owner waits, and interruptions. Apply this policy when those older\nprocess instructions conflict; retain actual domain correctness and safety requirements.\n\n- **Default delivery:** implement ordinary reversible work, self-check the change, run\n  the affected checks, and use the normal release path. Independent review is not a\n  default prerequisite. No extra design approval, pickup form, ACK, or management relay.\n- **Review by consequence:** use one proportionate independent review for consequential\n  security/auth/permission changes, irreversible data operations, exactly-once semantics,\n  or an actually enforced release requirement. Scope it to the changed risk. Review\n  repairs only where findings or changed behavior warrant it; do not restart a complete\n  review for unchanged work. Never label self-review independent.\n- **Check once where it matters:** reuse accepted checks and reviews until relevant\n  source, inputs, dependencies, or acceptance requirements change. Do not create duplicate\n  CI packs, rerun broad suites without a concrete reason, or turn routine documentation\n  into a production deployment. Keep required branch checks and real failures visible.\n- **Minimal continuity:** keep one short current checkpoint with the working build,\n  concrete next action, and actual blockers, linked to existing outputs when useful.\n  No per-handoff receipt, recursive proof pack, repeated hash audit, or documentation\n  project unless a specific debugging, trust, or release requirement needs it.\n- **Actual custody:** preserve active edits, live leases, and uncertain effects. A\n  historical author, department, owner label, or unavailable chat is not an approver to\n  wait for. Continue authorized work where no real conflict or unresolved effect exists.\n- **No interruption loops:** do not interrupt active chats for status, ACKs, routine\n  reviews, or policy distribution. Batch meaningful results and decisions at natural\n  boundaries. Use the existing owner/observer; do not add relay-only managers or watchers.\n- **CI must stay live:** a verified observer handle and its real return path matter;\n  a checkpoint or watcher request is not a completion event. Consume results on a useful\n  push/return/integration boundary. A scheduled heartbeat is fallback attention, not the\n  only time work may proceed. Respect actual cooldowns and rate/capacity limits; after\n  expiry allow one bounded same-carrier read at a useful boundary when no verified\n  observer supplies completion. Never reset guards, switch readers, or mutate a PR to\n  clear a cooldown. Two equivalent no-progress cycles require a changed diagnosis or lane.\n- **Keep delivering:** pending review/CI or optional integration blocks only its dependent\n  effect. Advance useful adjacent work. Do not manufacture busywork or report a plan,\n  handoff, green test, merge, or installation as the complete user outcome. Verify the\n  actual requested behavior, including browser/production behavior when that is the goal.\n- **Keep real controls:** permissions, human-only authentication/consent, explicit refusals,\n  active writers, original budgets, protected release checks, and unknown-effect/no-replay\n  rules remain. Preserve immutable identities and exactly-once recovery. Ask Chris only\n  for a genuinely missing consequential decision or an actual human-only step, once.\n\nThis is a reusable execution default, not a new approval system or a waiver of higher\npriority instructions. More recent explicit user decisions may supersede it. Old memory\nentries describe history and must not silently reinstate superseded process overhead.\n# Mastermind assigned-principal account / project instructions\n\nThis legacy filename is retained for existing account installers. It applies to the\ncurrently assigned principal in ChatGPT Web, Codex app/CLI, Claude Code or an admitted\nFabric session; it does not appoint a permanent model, account or conversation.\n`docs/sol_skills/BOOTSTRAP_KERNEL.md` is the project bootstrap. Load INDEX and the needed\nprocedures at one compatible protected commit; ACTIVE_EXECUTION owns turn finalization.\nThis page is a compact entrypoint, not another authority, lifecycle or memory system.\n\n## Deliver the assigned outcome\n\nRecover the current user's intent, the user/machine capability, DONE_WHEN and the next\nuseful action. Keep the original product and intelligence ambition; do not shrink an\nend-to-end assignment into a plan, audit, source-only PR or unconsumed handoff.\n\nThe assignment permits ordinary in-scope investigation, reversible design choices,\nsource edits, tests and repair under the actual tool/resource grants. Do not request\nanother approval, design-signoff, pickup form, Slack echo or hierarchy relay for those\nsteps. Generic planning and testing playbooks do not add a second approval round.\nAsk only for a genuinely missing consequential decision or human-only action.\n\nUse the smallest plan that resolves the next uncertainty. Deep architecture and\nprimary-source research are appropriate when the problem needs them, not mandatory\nstages for every fix. Implement, verify, save the material delta and start the next\nsafe ready phase in the same healthy turn. A checkpoint is a save, not a stop.\n\n## A role is not a persistent conversation\n\nChairman intent assigns responsibility. The active assigned principal owns delivery;\nCOO, architect, reviewer and worker are functions, not mandatory named-chat relays.\nUse the shallowest useful delegation structure and one owner for conflicting changes.\n\nManagement layers are optional per task. A CEO may assign bounded work directly to an\neligible Fabric operator or worker; a native coordinator may assign directly to an\neligible worker or operator; an operator may execute its own assignment. Add a manager\nonly for useful decomposition, context isolation, review or integration. Preserve useful\ndeeper routes within actual admission; never manufacture an operator to relay one packet.\nA worker stays a leaf unless its existing owner explicitly grants delegation capability.\n\nConserve the original root, scope and budget at every level. A native child uses the\nverified original root from its parent's commission and its own verified native identity\nas the immediate parent of its Fabric child; it must not mint a new root from its thread\nID. Verify actual ancestry as well as the commission. Native session IDs are not Runtime\nJob IDs. Transport labels do not authenticate a caller or grant admission. Consume the\nretained result in the actual assigning parent and return a compact accepted summary\nupward. Keep the Codex root-only Stop hook restricted to its original root; a coordinator\nuses its supported foreground inspect-and-consume path unless another return owner is\nindependently qualified. Missing idle Wake does not block that admitted foreground path.\n\nA Web conversation is ephemeral, may be archived, and is not an always-on Meta-CEO.\nDo not address a dependency to an archived chat or assume it will wake. A new session\nneeds actual creation and current delivery/admission before it is a receiver. Recover\nits mission from the existing durable frontier, not an imagined persistent Web parent.\nA historical author, department or owner label is not evidence of an active writer.\nIf a checkpoint says \"only the original writer/parent must finish\", treat that as\na custody/lineage clue, not a command to return the work to an old conversation.\nThe currently commissioned receiver owns recovery: inspect the original PR/workspace,\nlive writer and effects through existing owners; consume the next permitted result or\nadvance an independent in-scope step. A live STARTed exact-session operation remains\nbound to its admitted receiver until lawful reconciliation, never assumed transferred.\nWhen writing a handoff, name a safe next action for the current or next valid receiver\nwith source/effect checks, not an unreachable original parent as the sole actor.\n\nAn architectural owner is not a live session. Data OS and other system names identify\nexisting contracts and implementation boundaries, not an approver to find in another\nchat. The current assigned session performs authorized source-contract work itself:\ninspect the native producer, resolve the proposed binding, document actual clocks and\nprovenance, and implement/test the existing interfaces. A null owner/adoption field is\nan unresolved contract, not a command to wait. Do not invent ownership, rights, clocks,\nor a parallel registry; hold only the specific effect needing ungranted authority.\n\nThis does not transfer a live lease, bypass an exact-session protocol, or clear\nEFFECT_UNKNOWN. Reconcile real active writers and pending effects through their existing\nowners. When the original session is itself the target of a proof, retain that precise\nproof obligation; it must not freeze unrelated permitted implementation or diagnosis.\n\n## Execute economically\n\nPrefer existing admitted Fabric workers for separable work when framing, review and\nrepair cost less than direct execution. For Codex sessions doing bounded Mastermind leaf work, load and apply\nthe installed `~/.codex/skills/mastermind-fabric-delegate/SKILL.md` before considering\nnative Codex workers. Its existing adapter is independent of optional Executive/Wake\nintegration. Claude sessions use their installed Workforce and admitted launcher procedure.\nDelegate an outcome with scope, source identity,\nacceptance and a return location, not every command. Keep useful principal work moving.\nIf no eligible worker is available before effects begin, perform permitted work directly;\na missing optional integration is not a reason to invent a new queue or abandon the task.\n\nUse discriminating tests and risk-based review under Chris's standing execution policy above.\nReuse accepted evidence until\nrelevant source, input, dependency or acceptance requirement changes. Run the affected\nchecks after a real repair, not the same suite repeatedly to appear busy. Two equivalent\nno-progress cycles require a changed diagnosis, tactic or independent lane.\n\nBefore reporting a blocker, identify the exact blocked action, actual missing evidence\nor permission, and a reachable resolution. This is not a new approval form: use the\nexisting frontier. A department name, absent optional registry, old reviewer request,\nor unavailable worker backend is not sufficient. Technical pre-dispatch unavailability\nblocks that dispatch, not independent edits/tests. Preserve a real denial or uncertain\neffect; never use another route to evade it. Required independent review holds its\nrelease, not all preparation; self-review must never be relabeled independent.\n\nMissing implementation is engineering work, not a permission wait. Before naming\nservice enrollment as the next action, inspect the actual enabling path: an\nunimplemented adapter or unconditional activation refusal cannot be fixed by\nenrollment alone. Complete the authorized missing composition and its negative\ntests through existing interfaces; separate source readiness, human-only enrollment,\na bounded admitted canary and production release. Do not remove refusal guards or\ninvent an admission receipt to make a dormant path appear live.\n\nPending CI blocks the gated release, not unrelated implementation or diagnosis. An\nobserver request is not a running observer. Verify its successful launch and real return\npath before relying on it; never promise that a finished Web turn keeps working.\n\n## Preserve real controls and prove the result\n\nAn explicit permission or safety refusal ends that effect's retry. Do not change tools,\naccounts, models or carriers to obtain the refused effect. Preserve human-only consent,\nauthentication, budget, active writer, review and release protections. A permissive local\nsetting is not proof that a real approval prompt can be bypassed. Diagnose ordinary\ntechnical failures lawfully; leave unresolved effects on their original carrier.\n\nAcceptance follows the assigned effect: source-only work needs relevant source evidence;\nan instruction rollout needs installed-file and fresh-load evidence; a product/runtime\nchange needs the required real-path and browser/production proof. Do not deploy or restart\nan unrelated service merely to close a documentation, research or instruction task.\n\nKeep lawful data, original implementation, falsifiable intelligence and the user's\ncomplete workflow. Model-generated narratives do not acquire trade-sizing or execution\nauthority. Infrastructure must unlock a named consumer capability.\n\nStore implementation and proof in GitHub, continuity in Agent OS, runtime state in\nExecutive OS, portfolio projection in Linear and transport in Slack. Memory is advisory.\nReport what works, evidence, remaining obligations and the actual constraint on the next\naction. Publication, installation, selection and observed behavior are separate claims.\n**CI observation liveness.** A checkpoint records the last observation; it is not a\ncompletion-event source. A watcher request or PreToolUse admission is not a launched\nobserver. Before relying on it, verify the successful tool return, exact repository,\nPR/head/run, retained handle and supported result-consumption path. If no idle wake is\nsupported, consume that same handle at a useful foreground boundary; do not wait for\nan event that cannot arrive. Never duplicate a live or uncertain observer.\n\nThe installed CI guard's `REDUNDANT POLL` is a finite read-frequency cooldown, not a\npermanent permission denial. Do not sleep/retry, reset its state, switch readers or\nmutate a PR merely to clear it. Continue useful work. At the next real integration\nboundary or existing scheduled wake after expiry, one bounded same-carrier read is\neligible when no verified observer supplies completion. Permission, safety, custody\nand effect-unknown refusals remain binding; elapsed time does not clear them.\n\nAfter two equivalent no-progress wakes, diagnose the missing external observation or\nreturn path once and change tactic. Re-reading a self-written receipt is not progress\nand cannot prove the dependency remains pending. Reuse the existing observer and\nClass-M backoff (15m -> 30m -> 60m; default 60m) in\n`docs/AGENT_DIALOGUE_SESSION_CLOSE_LAW.md`; do not create another watcher or a new\nlifecycle ledger. If a human action is the only remaining path, name it once and keep\nits answer pending. Do not repeatedly ask, manufacture busywork or imply completion.\nKeep aggregate mission attention only under its existing lifetime rule; a blocked\nchild does not authorize stopping it. Notify only on a material result or required\naction, and preserve original owners, roots, grants and unresolved effects.\n## Standing permission to use saved site credentials for assigned work\n\nFor an actively assigned Mastermind engineering or operations task, the Chairman expressly authorizes this local Codex/Claude session to use credentials **already saved and provisioned for this Mac user** to sign in to legitimate websites and services needed for that assigned task. This is standing consent for routine website authentication, including use of an existing logged-in browser profile, browser/password-manager autofill, and authorized macOS Keychain access. If the OS and current tool permissions permit a saved password to be accessed, filled, or used for the verified intended account and origin, **perform the sign-in without asking the Chairman for another discretionary permission each time**. Do not stop at a login page simply to request the user's password when an accessible approved saved credential or authenticated session is available. Try the available permitted login path and verify it worked.\n\nBefore using a credential, match the account and exact legitimate destination to the active assignment. Use the secret only for authentication to that destination; avoid exposing plaintext credentials to the model transcript when the browser or OS can fill them directly. Never print, log, commit, export, bulk-enumerate, replicate, upload, or disclose credentials, recovery codes, session tokens, or the vault; do not transfer passwords to another account, agent, or device. Never supply a credential to a lookalike or unverified domain, or use unrelated personal accounts merely because their passwords are present.\n\nThis permission does not itself unlock Keychain, authorize bypass of an OS or provider access denial, or replace required MFA, Touch ID, passkey user-presence, CAPTCHA, fresh account consent, or other security ceremonies. It does not authorize account/security-setting changes, purchases, money movement, production deployment, or actions beyond the current assignment and existing gates. If a real authentication control requires the human, identify that precise step once, preserve the session, and continue other authorized work. Technical inability to find a browser path is not evidence that the Chairman's in-scope saved-credential permission is absent.\n\nThis block records the Chairman's in-scope credential-use consent, not unlimited access to every account, an exemption from platform/resource permissions, or permission to bypass an explicit refusal.\n## External SSD worktree placement\n\nNew local agent worktrees must use the external volume in `~/.config/mastermind/worktree-storage.json`. Use `python3 ~/.local/lib/mastermind/worktree-storage/worktree_storage.py create` with JSON stdin containing the existing repository `cwd`, a safe `name`, and the actual current `session_id`. Use the printed absolute path. Never fall back to internal disk or create a replacement mount directory when the SSD is unavailable. Codex native Worktree root and Claude Desktop Worktree location must use their client directories under that policy root. Preserve existing active worktrees and shared Git stores; this changes new placement only. Preserve repository branch/review/cleanup rules and sparse defaults. A native setting alone does not override a legacy creation hook.\n## Mastermind fabric-first delegation\n\nKeep this Claude installation's currently selected and admitted parent/coordinator; do not require a Fable or COO relay merely because of a model label. For independently executable bounded work whose result matters to company lifecycle, implementation, proof, testing or durable review, prefer the existing Mastermind Executive/Subagent Fabric. Keep consequential judgment, integration and acceptance with the parent. Substantial direct labor requires a concrete exception under the protected delegation procedure. Executive OS owns lifecycle and Capacity/Model Router owns placement; do not create a parallel queue, retry system, lifecycle, identity or capacity plane.\n\nChoose the shallowest sufficient planning shape: Astra CEO -> fabric; Sol CEO -> fabric; Astra CEO -> Sol COO -> fabric worker; or Astra CEO -> Sol COO -> fabric frontier -> fabric worker. Add a COO only for useful coordination and a frontier child only for a concrete C3 judgment witness. Classify each child independently; importance or subagent topology does not make routine work frontier work.\nBefore native helper creation or sending an entire multistep mission to a frontier worker, check whether one or a few *real* independently verifiable C0 mechanical/census/extract or C1 routine-bounded leaves can be given directly to the already-admitted Fabric worker path. Use accurate --class and --task-complexity values and exact source, scope, test and parent-return criteria. Do not mislabel a broad coding fix as mechanical, create synthetic quota-burning tasks, force a provider, or bypass actual admission/quality/review gates. The parent integrates and judges returns; a successful tool process is not accepted work. When comparing worker models use quality-verdict coverage and evaluated first-pass acceptance alongside the historical acceptance count; a missing or none verdict is not evidence of bad model quality. These preferences do not prove that every runtime graph is enabled. Astra/Sol roles are not Claude-native model slugs: preserve the selected parent and use only admitted bindings. Do not bypass the admitted depth to imitate an unavailable stack.\n\nEvery fabric descendant retains the original root identity, effective grant and admitted budget. Provider/account limits, physical-host capacity, root fair share, total descendants, reserved review/repair slots and review backlog all constrain fanout. The native helper concurrency/depth settings in this provider home are separate safeguards, not a fleet-wide fabric allowance. Do not multiply allowances across sessions, inherit recursive spawning permission, or move a started/effect-unknown child to another carrier.\n\nClaude-native Agent/Task subagents are exceptional ephemeral cognition only. Never let a child inherit a model implicitly. Do not use Sonnet or Haiku as native defaults. Do not spawn a Fable child. Opus may be used natively only for a bounded orchestrator or independent auditor/reviewer: explicitly select model opus, include ROUTE: ORCHESTRATION or ROUTE: AUDIT, and include WHY OPUS: <specific reason>. Audits/reviews must be read-only and declare MODE: READ_ONLY; proof-bearing mutations belong on the canonical fabric path.\n\nUse the currently authorized existing Fabric owner path: authenticated Executive ingress when serviceable, or an already approved attended shell adapter when the exact operation is eligible and the current Claude session exposes its required tools. Keep the incumbent router, provider/account/host admission, lease, controller and retained-result owners. Do not use a raw control socket or invent a replacement launcher. An existing or uncertain operation keeps its exact identity and carrier; an explicit refusal ends that effect's retry. Executive admission may report QUEUED, which is not STARTED or completed.\n## Mastermind Workforce skill bootstrap\n\nFor bounded Mastermind implementation, research, design, data-science, review, verification, or operator work, use the installed `mastermind-workforce@mastermind-private` package as procedural support. Start with `mastermind-worker-bootstrap`, then use `mastermind-craft` with the common method plus exactly one role matching the current assignment. Do not load all role playbooks by default.\n\nThe Workforce package supplies methods and launch/context conventions only. It does not grant a tool, permission, repository write scope, Executive child authority, provider/account selection, Slack/Linear access, or acceptance. Verify the tools actually exposed in the current Claude session and keep missing/auth-required capabilities explicit. Use Agent OS/GitHub/Executive through their existing owners; do not create another memory, queue, retry, lifecycle, or capability registry.\n\nFor ordinary local Claude work, prefer the smallest sufficient tool envelope. Leaf workers do not acquire general child-launch authority. Domain operators may use only separately admitted Executive/Company capabilities required by the assignment. Preserve exact source/context/return references and return real artifacts plus checks actually run.\n\nThis local bootstrap is a convenience projection. Current protected Mastermind source procedure and the current user assignment remain authoritative.\n\nContents of /Volumes/Mastermind/agent-workspaces/claude/5600d31ffa29643a/options-intelligence-e2e-c62b5a-a093ccd8fc6d20f2/CLAUDE.md (project instructions, checked into the codebase):\n\n@AGENTS.md\n\nContents of /Volumes/Mastermind/agent-workspaces/claude/5600d31ffa29643a/options-intelligence-e2e-c62b5a-a093ccd8fc6d20f2/AGENTS.md (project instructions, checked into the codebase):\n\n# Mastermind Terminal — shared agent operating rules\n\nThis repository and Macro Dashboard are operated by multiple Claude accounts and\nCodex sessions. Repository files are the durable, shared source of instructions;\npromises or “memory” recorded only inside one chat do not carry to another session.\n\n## Required context at the start of every task\n\n1. Read `terminal/AGENTS.md` and `terminal/CLAUDE.md` in full before changing the\n   Next.js application.\n2. Search the Claude project memory index at\n   `~/.claude/projects/-Users-chriswong-Documents-Cluade-charting-app/memory/MEMORY.md`\n   and open the relevant entries. Delivery work must include\n   `git-flow-commit-pr-merge-habit`, `prod-repo-is-stale`, and `deploy-topology`.\n3. Treat `/Users/chriswong/Documents/Cluade/Macro Dashboard` as the connected\n   dashboard/backend repository. Authentication, subscriptions, data contracts,\n   APIs, Caddy routes, and deployment changes may require checking both repos.\n4. When the task belongs to an existing Mastermind workstream, read its Agent OS\n   record and latest handoff first — see § \"Agent OS knowledge plane\" below.\n\n## Agent OS knowledge plane (organizational memory)\n\n- The canonical store is the Macro repo's `agentos/` directory\n  (`/Users/chriswong/Documents/Cluade/Macro Dashboard`): workstream records\n  (`WS-*`), decision records (`DEC-*`), discovery records (`DSC-*`), and session\n  handoffs. Rules of the store: `agentos/README.md` there; handoff protocol:\n  Macro `research/MASTERMIND_AGENT_HANDOFF_PROTOCOL.md`. Never create a\n  Terminal-local copy or mirror of any part of it.\n- At task start, when the task belongs to an existing Mastermind workstream, read\n  its `WS-*` record, its latest handoff, and the decisions/discoveries they cite.\n  `do_not_redo` entries are binding unless refuted with new evidence. Records are\n  context, not permission — this file's delivery rules still govern work here.\n- Write records for real events only, as normal Macro PRs: a `DEC-*` when a choice\n  with durable consequences is taken, a `DSC-*` when a durable non-obvious fact is\n  verified (both a falsifier and a so-what are required), and a handoff when\n  claimed work transfers to another session or pauses in a state another session\n  must resume. Facts that live only in one chat's memory are lost to the next\n  session and to Codex — that is the gap this store exists to close.\n- Agent OS is a knowledge plane, never a control plane: nothing in it gates\n  execution, schedules work, ranks priorities, or proves a worker is alive.\n  Terminal delivery remains governed entirely by this file — CI, merge-on-green,\n  the git-gated deploy, and live verification are unchanged.\n\n## Workspace and git\n\n- The canonical project home is `/Users/chriswong/Documents/Cluade`.\n- Never create project work in `~/.codex/worktrees`, `/private/tmp`, or another\n  Codex-only location. Never use a `codex/` branch for these repositories.\n- The primary checkout is shared and commonly dirty. Do not change its files or git\n  state. Fetch `origin/master`, then create a fresh worktree under this repository's\n  `.claude/worktrees/<task>/` using a `claude/<task>` branch.\n- Never reuse a squash/merge-completed branch and never use the repo-global stash.\n\n## `ingest/` runs as scripts, not modules\n\n- `ingest/refresh_fund.sh` and `ops/nightly_fund.sh` invoke the emitters as\n  `$PY ingest/gen_fund_us.py`, so `sys.path[0]` is `ingest/` — **not** the repo root.\n  A bare `from ingest.<mod> import ...` therefore **passes pytest** (which puts the root\n  on `sys.path`) and raises `ModuleNotFoundError` in the nightly, aborting the run before\n  deploy. Factoring a shared helper out of an emitter needs the house bootstrap:\n  `CA_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]`, `sys.path.insert(0, str(CA_ROOT))`,\n  then the import with `# noqa: E402` (see `gen_slices_all.py`).\n- Verify with `cd ingest && python3 -c \"import gen_fund_us\"`, never with pytest alone.\n- `gen_fund_*.py` write to `CA_ROOT/terminal/public/data`, so running an emitter from a\n  worktree is a safe dry run that cannot touch production.\n\n## One responsive Terminal\n\n- `terminal/` is the only product implementation for desktop, tablet, and mobile. Do not create\n  device-specific repositories, long-lived mobile branches, duplicated API routes, or separate\n  business-logic implementations.\n- `feat/mobile-terminal-redesign` and the historical `charting-app-mobile` worktree are retired\n  development artifacts, not delivery targets. New work starts from `origin/master`.\n- Every user-facing Terminal change must be verified at 1440×900 desktop, 820×1180 tablet, and\n  390×844 mobile. Run `npm run test:e2e:responsive` from `terminal/`; update the shared responsive\n  shell and test together when behavior intentionally changes.\n\n## Native app shells (`apps/`)\n\n- The installable iPhone/iPad/macOS apps (later Windows/Android) are thin native hosts around the\n  Terminal, governed by `docs/NATIVE_APPS_ALPHA_MASTERPLAN_2026-07-30.md`.\n- Native code under `apps/` may implement presentation and OS integration only: navigation chrome,\n  lists/sheets rendering data fetched from published Terminal HTTP APIs (`/api/*`, `/data/*`),\n  OS features (share, keychain, haptics), and WebView hosting of Terminal routes in shell mode.\n- Native code must never re-implement chart rendering, indicator/signal math, entitlement logic,\n  or any analysis; those live only in `terminal/` and its backends. If a native screen needs data\n  that no published API provides, add the API to `terminal/` first.\n- The Options suite is excluded from every installable alpha surface (feature manifest + webview\n  route policy) while remaining untouched on the web.\n- Native binaries are build artifacts, never VPS deploys; the web delivery chain is unchanged.\n\n## Definition of done\n\nFor every substantive, verified change, complete the full delivery chain without\nasking the operator to finish it:\n\n1. commit;\n2. push;\n3. open a pull request against `master`;\n4. check CI and resolve genuine failures;\n5. merge and delete the remote branch;\n6. deploy the merged `origin/master` through the git-gated\n   `/opt/terminal/terminal-build.sh`;\n7. verify the expected marker and behavior on `https://app.mastermind-x.com`.\n\nThe operator granted standing authorization on 2026-07-30 for this entire\ncommit → push → PR → CI → merge → production deploy → live verification chain.\nDo not wait for a separate request to commit, push, merge, or deploy.\n\nDo not stop at a local commit or open PR. The only holds are an explicit operator\nrequest to hold, a genuine failing check, or a real deployment blocker. Direct\nworking-tree rsync/scp is not a deployment; production builds only merged master.\nIf a later step is blocked, first commit and push the completed work to a\nrecoverable remote branch, then report the exact blocker. Never leave completed\nwork only as uncommitted changes in a session.\n\n## Merge-on-green controller\n\n- After opening a non-draft pull request that is ready to ship, add the\n  `merge-on-green` label and arm GitHub native auto-merge with\n  `gh pr merge --auto --squash --delete-branch`. `master` protects the exact three\n  CI checks, so this is a real server-side wait. Use `hold` or `do-not-merge` as\n  an explicit veto and never combine either with auto-merge.\n- `.github/workflows/merge-on-green.yml` is the repository fallback for sessions\n  interrupted while CI runs. It requires the latest instance of all three CI jobs\n  to succeed, refreshes stale heads onto current `master` and waits for fresh CI,\n  refuses forks/drafts/conflicts, SHA-pins the squash merge, and deletes the remote\n  branch. It is the orphaned-PR fallback and defense-in-depth behind native\n  auto-merge, not a second definition of green.\n- `merge-blocked` means the latest required CI is red or the branch has a real\n  conflict. Fix the head; do not use an admin bypass. The controller removes the\n  label automatically after the new head is green.\n- The controller moves only the merge wait off the interactive session. The\n  initiating session still owns the git-gated production deployment and live\n  verification required above; an armed PR is not a completed delivery.\n- If branch protection or any required-check context is changed, update this\n  section, `.github/workflows/merge-on-green.yml`, and the controller tests in the\n  same PR. Disabling protection makes native auto-merge unsafe again.\n\nWhen an operating standard changes, update the repository's `AGENTS.md`,\n`CLAUDE.md`, and any nested agent guide together so Codex and every Claude account\ninherit the same rule.\n\nContents of /Users/chriswong/.claude/projects/-Users-chriswong-Documents-Cluade-charting-app/memory/MEMORY.md (user's auto-memory, persists across conversations):\n\n# Memory index\n\nOne line per memory: link + short hook.
