The v1 and adjacent artifact byte hashes and row counts were re-read at this head; the h60 digest in the census table below is corrected to its full 64-hex value. No implementation claim follows from this refresh.\n15\t\n16\tThe protected Mastermind procedure used for the commissioning and review boundary was Skillpack commit:\n17\t\n18\t`0471cea4f891da1ec0c9fbeff10a9391f9cdd90f`\n19\t\n20\tThe Skillpack index at that commit is `mastermind.sol_skillpack.v1`, version `1.0.1`, minimum bootstrap major `1`. The v1 context validator remains byte-pinned. Its `_MAX_REFERENCES` is still `4_096`; the independent auditor retains its historical unpinned `25_000` row / `48 MiB` read ceiling; the service remains `TimeoutStartSec=180` and `CPUQuota=50%`.\n21\t\n22\tPR #6691 was the incumbent records-only carrier at head `8b4136575be759a53c9f6fe7d7d104cc9c2af572`. It was squash-merged on 2026-09-24T07:57:34Z (re-checked 2026-10-11), so its owner correction is on `main`. This packet does not edit its files, replace it, or duplicate its owner correction.\n23\t\n24\tNo implementation PR, shadow validator, second audit authority, trusted-context recoupling, owner eviction, window, truncation, timeout increase, CPU increase, or v1 byte change is authorized by this packet.\n25\t\n26\t## Corpus census at the design SHA (historical)\n27\t\n28\tThe census was performed from immutable blobs at the exact Macro source SHA above. These are measurements, not a future growth forecast. They are superseded as capacity evidence by the capacity refresh below.\n29\t\n30\t| v1 input or adjacent artifact | rows | bytes | SHA-256 | v1 treatment |\n31\t|---|---:|---:|---|---|\n32\t| `data/options_signal_episode/episodes.jsonl` | 9,641 | 15,013,207 | `20120ddeff5983248ec404afaa65d3414c36ed1011936638c84e4ada52f50940` | consumed |\n33\t| `data/options_signal_episode/outcomes_h60.jsonl` | 7,843 | 15,886,486 | `18fb5dd5deb5114b1d55778eb6c3a4632207d58fa0aead63154653b4f7d7d67e` | consumed |\n34\t| `data/options_signal_episode/campaigns.jsonl` | 8 | 10,492 | `db326f5c772ab417c43b8579ad50abb0434916922bda3a13c2da5b8303813910` | frozen legacy campaign input |\n35\t| `config/market_memory_canary.v1.json` | — | 1,650 | `5e7823e48866b2c0828122b65f684ed5872c6816a6224f61e44db4c03d129b33` | consumed |\n36\t| `data/options_signal_episode/outcomes_session.jsonl` | 30,327 | 100,471,221 | measured, not bound here | adjacent artifact; explicit exclusion required |\n37\t| `data/options_signal_campaign/campaigns.jsonl` | 8,385 | 17,571,990 | `0c242957703d4a3a0031f0bf7322195c6d41f665391380e30626aea31bf95514` | separate campaign-v2 selector artifact; not a v1 context source |\n38\t\n39\tThe v1 context audit therefore sees approximately 29.48 MiB of source bytes and approximately 9,649 owner references (9,641 episode owners plus eight frozen campaign owners), already 2.36 times the pinned 4,096-reference ceiling. The old handoff measurement of 3,897 episode rows is stale.\n40\t\n41\tThe corpus is bursty: episode `available_at` spans 15 calendar days with a maximum daily burst of 2,197 rows; h60 computation spans 12 days with a maximum daily burst of 1,853 rows. A linear forecast from this snapshot is not defensible. Resource limits must be established by a bounded benchmark and a stated growth envelope.\n42\t\n43\tThe two campaign paths are semantically distinct. The legacy eight-row path is frozen and consumed by v1. The larger `data/options_signal_campaign/campaigns.jsonl` path is used by the v2 selector machinery. Its companion `data/options_signal_campaign/outcomes.jsonl` is also an adjacent owner ledger (28,423 rows / 67,324,077 bytes) and is not in the v1 source set. A v2 audit must carry a same-snapshot source manifest that explicitly classifies every owner-producing artifact as consumed, superseded, or excluded with a reason and contract reference. It may not silently omit either live campaign-v2 artifact or silently merge them into the legacy path.\n44\t\n45\t### Frozen source-manifest decision\n46\t\n47\tThe v2 context-audit owner population is the complete set of episode owners plus the context references reachable from the registered campaign contract. `data/options_signal_episode/episodes.jsonl` is the owner ledger; `data/options_signal_episode/campaigns.jsonl` is the frozen legacy campaign reference ledger. `data/options_signal_episode/outcomes_h60.jsonl` and `config/market_memory_canary.v1.json` are consumed dependency artifacts.\n48\t\n49\t`data/options_signal_campaign/campaigns.jsonl`, `data/options_signal_campaign/outcomes.jsonl`, `data/options_signal_episode/outcomes_session.jsonl`, and `data/options_signal_campaign/checkpoint.json` are adjacent derivation/dependency artifacts, not independent context owners. They must nevertheless be included in the immutable source-generation manifest with path, digest, bytes, rows, checkpoint identity, and an explicit exclusion reason. Any change to one of these excluded inputs between the bound source snapshot and validation forces a new generation or a typed refusal; an excluded artifact may never mutate silently while the audit reports healthy. The live campaign-v2 path is therefore not silently enumerated as complete and is not silently merged with the frozen legacy path.\n50\t\n51\tThe current live campaign output is stale: its durable checkpoint binds an 8,872-row episode prefix while the episode ledger contains 9,641 rows, its campaign writer commit predates the latest episode writer by four days, and 769 current episodes have no campaign member. The v2 audit must return `CAMPAIGN_OUTPUT_STALE` / `DERIVATION_PENDING` for that state rather than re-deriving campaigns or manufacturing owner coverage.\n52\t\n53\t## Capacity refresh at current main (2026-10-11)\n54\t\n55\tThe design-SHA census above is historical. The governed corpus roughly tripled after it was taken. Re-measured from immutable blobs at Macro `origin/main` `f8dc4bb05eceb4e2be0f1e1e5c6c87e12a2e9b07` (rows = newline count, bytes = blob size):\n56\t\n57\t| artifact | rows | bytes | SHA-256 | rows at design SHA |\n58\t|---|---:|---:|---|---:|\n59\t| `data/options_signal_episode/episodes.jsonl` | 29,509 | 45,982,874 | `a7c53ed59a9fd268470f9b107c8b9665707e31192e23331d2ad9e736f9270652` | 9,641 |\n60\t| `data/options_signal_episode/outcomes_h60.jsonl` | 25,338 | 50,889,496 | `617039e5e1a45f9e9a70682060e156b8e778a49c58a85d0a96bd89f8f4c69fa4` | 7,843 |\n61\t| `data/options_signal_episode/campaigns.jsonl` | 8 | 10,492 | `db326f5c772ab417c43b8579ad50abb0434916922bda3a13c2da5b8303813910` | 8 |\n62\t| `data/options_signal_episode/outcomes_session.jsonl` | 30,327 | 100,471,221 | `fc02c3f6d224ada2179f89fcf09d63866567e4132d6d6738ccc90c012c9b6311` | 30,327 |\n63\t| `data/options_signal_campaign/campaigns.jsonl` | 8,385 | 17,571,990 | `0c242957703d4a3a0031f0bf7322195c6d41f665391380e30626aea31bf95514` | 8,385 |\n64\t| `data/options_signal_campaign/outcomes.jsonl` | 28,423 | 67,324,077 | `bfde356d4e54265164840e46f06e391afbed3c35449cda4786013eb95f432160` | 28,423 |\n65\t\n66\tEpisode `session_date` spans 2026-08-10 to 2026-09-25 (28 sessions); the newest episode row belongs to session 2026-09-25. The last five sessions carried 2026-09-21: 5,598; 2026-09-22: 2,161; 2026-09-23: 2,280; 2026-09-24: 2,319; 2026-09-25: 2,544 rows. These counts show the observed production rate while the producer is active; they are not a forecast.\n67\t\n68\tThe current corpus already exceeds every capacity figure the incumbent stack carries. These refusals are correct fail-closed behaviour, not defects to route around:\n69\t\n70\t1. v1 `_MAX_REFERENCES = 4_096` (`engine/options_market_memory_context.py`): 29,517 owner references (29,509 episode owners plus eight frozen campaign owners) is 7.2 times the pinned ceiling.\n71\t2. The independent auditor's historical 25,000-row / 48 MiB read ceiling: episodes 29,509 rows > 25,000; h60 50,889,496 bytes > 48 MiB (50,331,648 bytes).\n72\t3. `MAX_SOURCE_ROWS = 25_000` in `engine/neuralweb/market_memory_production_records.py`: the production-records capture refuses with \"owner source artifact exceeds its row bound\" on the live corpus (observed on the production host 2026-10-10 and 2026-10-11).\n73\t\n74\tWhether any of these bounds moves is a preregistration contract decision for the commissioning seat, recorded as a `DEC:` record. This charter raises none of them and never passes one by windowing, eviction, or truncation (`DNR:KILL-OPTIONS-CONTEXT-AUDIT-OWNER-EVICTION`).\n75\t\n76\t### Declared growth envelope (proposed for seat acceptance)\n77\t\n78\tThe stale 25,000-row / 48 MiB figure is replaced by a stated envelope, not by silence:\n79\t\n80\t- **Design capacity:** the acceptance fixture covers at least four times the refreshed corpus — 118,036 episode rows / 183,931,496 bytes and 101,352 h60 rows / 203,557,984 bytes — with the adjacent artifacts scaled by the same factor.\n81\t- **Horizon statement:** the implementation's benchmark receipt states its measured in-envelope ceiling and the date the observed production rate reaches it. At the median recent rate of 2,319 episode rows per active session, four times the current corpus is about 39 active sessions away; a design whose ceiling is weeks out must say so in its receipt.\n82\t- **Past the ceiling:** an input above the measured ceiling produces a `RESOURCE_LIMIT` refusal — visible and receipted — never a kill, a truncated audit, or a healthy empty result.\n83\t- **Construction freedom, fixed budget:** within the fixed service class the implementation may meet the envelope with a full scan or with prefix-incremental generations (each run's work bounded by the appended suffix, the receipt chain binding every prior prefix digest). The choice is the implementation child's; the budget is not.\n84\t\n85\t## Existing algorithm and measured lower bound\n86\t\n87\tThe current v1 path performs these stages:\n88\t\n89\t1. Read complete JSONL ledgers and the canary configuration.\n90\t2. Decode and validate every episode and outcome row.\n91\t3. Authenticate the frozen eight-row legacy campaign ledger.\n92\t4. Resolve episode and campaign context references.\n93\t5. Sort references by owner schema and owner identifier.\n94\t6. Canonicalize JSON with stable key ordering, hash the reference set, and write immutable reference-set and audit objects followed by an atomic receipt HEAD.\n95\t\n96\tMeasured local timings over the current source snapshot were approximately 0.034 seconds to parse the legacy campaign ledger, 0.796 seconds to parse episodes, 0.840 seconds to parse h60 outcomes, 1.972 seconds for episode contract validation, and 13.478 seconds for campaign replay. The full context-store resolution and final receipt timing were not measurable because the W1A/trusted context stores are not present in the sparse checkout.\n97\t\n98\tA complete-population audit has a hard lower bound of Ω(N) input work and Ω(bytes) hashing work. Every governed row and every bound source artifact must be read, parsed, identity-checked, clock-checked, and authenticated. An index-only or sampled path cannot prove completeness. The v2 objective is bounded memory and truthful I/O, not sublinear full-corpus work.\n99\t\n100\tThe current campaign replay is itself a refusal input: it derives 110 campaigns with seven pending from the current episode/outcome corpus and does not equal the frozen eight-row legacy ledger. The live campaign checkpoint binds only an 8,872-row episode prefix while the episode ledger has 9,641 rows; 769 episodes therefore have no campaign member, and the latest campaign formation (2026-09-03) trails the latest episode (2026-09-04). v2 must bind a same-snapshot checkpoint and preserve missing/latest-prefix rows as a typed source/replay refusal until a separately accepted campaign contract resolves them; it must not silently rewrite the legacy ledger or treat replay equality as a best-effort warning.\n101\t\n102\tCurrent append writers provide canonical-byte idempotency and reject conflicting IDs or semantic keys under an inode lock and fsync. They do not provide correction or revision lineage. That missing lineage is a v2 contract requirement.\n103\t\n104\t## Chosen v2 construction strategy\n105\t\n106\tThe implementation child must use bounded streaming with deterministic external-memory canonicalization:\n107\t\n108\t1. **Freeze the source snapshot.** Record source commit/ref, exact path manifest, source device/inode identity where available, byte size, row count, source hash, required append-prefix hash, freshness clock, generation, and the future NYSE session boundary before reading.\n109\t2. **Enumerate without sampling.** Enumerate every source path declared by the governed source manifest. For each path, either consume it or emit an explicit contract-backed exclusion. Missing, unexpected, or unclassified owner sources fail closed.\n110\t3. **Stream each input once.** Parse JSONL in bounded chunks while computing the full source hash, validating schema, clocks, identity and append-prefix invariants. The scanner must detect shrink, reorder, backdated insertion, in-place mutation, malformed rows, duplicate semantic keys, and source drift between start and end.\n111\t4. **Emit bounded sorted runs.** Write content-addressed temporary runs keyed by canonical owner/group key with stable tie-breakers `(available_at, episode_id, source_row)` or the exact schema-defined equivalent. Run metadata includes digest, row count, byte count, ordering identifier, and source generation.\n112\t5. **Deterministically merge.** Perform a k-way merge with stable run-digest/path tie-breaking. Reject duplicate semantic owners with conflicting payloads. Validate one-to-one source/reference counts and exact legacy/v2 owner joins during the merge.\n113\t6. **Stream the final set.** Emit canonical reference bytes in the frozen owner order, compute the complete reference-set hash, and write the receipt only after all validation passes. Publish the receipt atomically; no partial receipt or health claim is valid.\n114\t\n115\tIf a source is proven append-ordered by a checked invariant, a one-pass fast path may avoid external sorting. The same invariant must be verified on every run; otherwise the implementation must use the external merge path or refuse. The fast path is an optimization, not a separate authority.\n116\t\n117\tThe strategy costs O(N) input and output I/O and O(N log N) comparisons in the external-sort path. Peak memory is bounded by the configured run chunk. Temporary disk is bounded by the run manifest and high-water limit. No row, owner, correction, or source may be evicted to satisfy those limits.\n118\t\n119\t## v2 receipt and schema contract\n120\t\n121\tThe new v2 schema, builder, and receipt are a new preregistration triad. v1 remains byte-frozen and independent. The receipt must bind:\n122\t\n123\t- schema and preregistration version;\n124\t- operation ID and immutable source commit/ref;\n125\t- future NYSE session boundary and freshness policy;\n126\t- complete source manifest, including consumed and explicitly excluded artifacts;\n127\t- per-artifact path, digest, byte count, record count, generation, and prefix digest where required;\n128\t- canonical owner/reference ordering identifier;\n129\t- algorithm identifier and source parser/validator contract digests;\n130\t- run manifest with run digests, run count, rows, bytes, ordering and temporary-disk high-water;\n131\t- observed peak RSS, CPU seconds, wall seconds, input/output bytes, and receipt bytes;\n132\t- reference-set digest and canonical count;\n133\t- per-source counts of rows accepted as prospective, classified retrospective, abstained, or refused, broken down by reason code, so an all-abstain or all-retrospective run is visible in the receipt itself (nulls printed, never implied);\n134\t- correction/replay lineage (`supersedes`, old/new source hashes, and generation IDs);\n135\t- typed refusal code when no receipt is emitted.\n136\t\n137\tThe future NYSE boundary resets the forward cohort. Rows before that boundary are retrospective or abstain according to the frozen contract; delayed observation cannot make them prospective. A rule, source, validator, or schema change requires a new version and a new forward cohort.\n138\t\n139\t## Corrections, replay, and session boundary\n140\t\n141\tA successful receipt is immutable. A correction that changes already committed source bytes creates a new generation with an explicit supersedes link and exact old/new hashes; it never overwrites the old receipt. A backdated insertion, source shrink, reorder, prefix mismatch, conflicting duplicate, or source mutation during a scan discards uncommitted runs and returns a typed refusal.\n142\t\n143\tA crash before receipt commit may replay the same operation against the same immutable source snapshot. The replay must produce byte-identical runs, reference bytes, and receipt content. A changed source or changed operation/session identity is a new generation, not a retry of the old effect.\n144\t\n145\tThe implementation must define the freshness clock, stale-source policy, session-start and session-end checks, and the exact relationship between the source generation and the future NYSE boundary before implementation begins. The boundary rule is frozen as: the first NYSE session open after the complete v2 schema, builder, validator, receipt triad, and its exact source-generation contract are accepted on `origin/main`; no row may be backdated into that cohort.\n146\t\n147\tA cross-file snapshot is mandatory. Stable-read checks on individual files are insufficient: the implementation must bind one source generation/checkpoint across episodes, outcomes, campaign ledgers, campaign outcomes, and canary/config inputs, then refuse if any file or checkpoint moves between reads.\n148\t\n149\t## Resource envelope and refusal contract\n150\t\n151\tThe implementation child must benchmark the corpus as re-measured in the capacity refresh above (or a newer re-measurement at its own source head) and the declared growth envelope before freezing numeric limits. The benchmark must measure at least:\n152\t\n153\t- input rows and bytes per source;\n154\t- JSON parse and validation CPU seconds;\n155\t- reference construction CPU seconds;\n156\t- sorting/merge CPU seconds and run count;\n157\t- peak RSS;\n158\t- temporary-disk high-water and available-space floor;\n159\t- canonical reference-set bytes, audit bytes, receipt bytes and HEAD bytes;\n160\t- wall time under the actual service class.\n161\t\n162\tLimits must include measured headroom and separate row and byte ceilings for episodes and h60. The historical eight-megabyte h60 ceiling cannot be reused blindly because the current h60 artifact is already approximately 15.9 MB. Numeric limits are implementation acceptance inputs, not chosen in this charter without the missing benchmark.\n163\t\n164\tThe implementation must fail closed, emit no receipt, and leave no accepted health claim on:\n165\t\n166\t- incomplete or unclassified owner population;\n167\t- missing, malformed, stale, mutated, reordered, shrunk, or hash-mismatched source;\n168\t- legacy/v2 campaign contract mismatch;\n169\t- duplicate semantic owner or conflicting duplicate;\n170\t- source/reference count mismatch;\n171\t- nondeterministic run or merge output;\n172\t- context-generation or source-prefix drift;\n173\t- correction without a new generation;\n174\t- RSS, CPU, wall-time, input-byte, output-byte, or temporary-disk limit breach;\n175\t- receipt/reference-set/HEAD size overflow;\n176\t- replay that is not byte-identical.\n177\t\n178\tRefusal codes must be machine-readable, stable, and included in the implementation acceptance tests. Failures must not be swallowed, windowed, sampled, truncated, evicted, or converted into a healthy empty result.\n179\tThe initial refusal vocabulary is frozen: `SOURCE_MANIFEST_INCOMPLETE`, `SOURCE_SNAPSHOT_UNSTABLE`, `SOURCE_MUTATED`, `MALFORMED_ROW`, `PREFIX_MISMATCH`, `CHECKPOINT_STALE`, `CAMPAIGN_OUTPUT_STALE`, `DERIVATION_PENDING`, `CAMPAIGN_REPLAY_MISMATCH`, `OWNER_MISSING`, `DUPLICATE_OWNER_CONFLICT`, `CORRECTION_LINEAGE_MISSING`, `DEPENDENCY_MISMATCH`, `BOUNDARY_INVALID`, `NONDETERMINISTIC_OUTPUT`, `REPLAY_MISMATCH`, `RESOURCE_LIMIT`, `RECEIPT_OVERFLOW`, and `AUTHORITY_VIOLATION`. Each refusal records the operation ID, source-generation ID, per-source digests/counts, checkpoint IDs, and the first failing stage.\n180\t\n181\t### Refusal before kill: the service envelope is fixed\n182\t\n183\t`app/deploy/macro-market-memory-options-context-audit.service` carries `TimeoutStartSec=180`, `CPUQuota=50%`, `MemoryHigh=256M`, `MemoryMax=512M`, `Nice=10`, and `IOWeight=20`; v2 raises none of them. `DSC:OPTIONS-CONTEXT-AUDIT-V1-TIMEOUT-PRECEDES-4096-REFUSAL` records that v1's O(N) construction is killed by the timeout before its own 4,096-reference refusal can fire. A kill leaves no receipt and no refusal code, which makes it indistinguishable from a run that never started. v2 must invert that ordering:\n184\t\n185\t1. **In-process limits fire first.** Every resource limit is enforced inside the process and trips before its systemd counterpart: wall budget at most 150 seconds (a 30-second margin under `TimeoutStartSec=180` to serialize the refusal record); CPU budgeting that treats `CPUQuota=50%` as halving available CPU (a stage measured at T unthrottled CPU-seconds costs about 2T wall-seconds); RSS budget at most 224 MiB, below `MemoryHigh=256M` (throttling) and well below `MemoryMax=512M` (OOM kill).\n186\t2. **Checked where the work is.** Budgets are checked at every stage boundary and at bounded intervals inside each parse, run-emit, and merge loop, so an over-budget input ends in `RESOURCE_LIMIT` with the first failing stage recorded — not in a timeout, a throttle stall, or an OOM kill.\n187\t3. **The disposable benchmark already crosses one line.** Its peak RSS of 274,907,136 bytes (about 262 MiB, macOS `ru_maxrss`) was measured on the design-SHA corpus and already exceeds `MemoryHigh`. The implementation benchmark must run under the real service class (the unit itself on a test host, or `systemd-run` with the same properties) at both the refreshed corpus and the growth envelope.\n188\t4. **Acceptance.** An over-envelope fixture run under the real service class yields a `RESOURCE_LIMIT` refusal record and an exit status distinct from success; journal evidence shows no SIGKILL, timeout, or OOM. This extends acceptance test 6.\n189\t\n190\t## Binding constraints (do not redo)\n191\t\n192\tThese bind the charter and the implementation child. Each is a closed decision; reopening one requires a material invalidator recorded by the commissioning seat.\n193\t\n194\t- No widening of `_MAX_REFERENCES` (`engine/options_market_memory_context.py:43` stays `4_096`; v1 stays byte-pinned by `research/options_estate/sparse_selector_preregistration_receipt_v1.json`).\n195\t- No raise of `TimeoutStartSec`, `CPUQuota`, `MemoryHigh`, or `MemoryMax` on the audit unit.\n196\t- No owner eviction, windowing, rotation, sampling, or truncation (`DNR:KILL-OPTIONS-CONTEXT-AUDIT-OWNER-EVICTION`).\n197\t- No swallowing: a refusal exits visibly and is never converted into a healthy empty result.\n198\t- No shadow validator and no second audit authority.\n199\t- No recoupling into `macro-market-memory-context.service`: the W2C context owner stays decoupled from the options audit (`DEC:W2C-V1-CONTEXT-OWNER-DECOUPLED-FROM-OPTIONS-AUDIT`); recoupling re-breaks W2C.\n200\t- No v2 implementation inside a W2C pull request.\n201\t\n202\t## Acceptance tests for the implementation child\n203\t\n204\tThe later implementation PR is acceptable only when an independent reviewer can verify all of the following against its exact source head:\n205\t\n206\t1. **Complete-population coverage:** the source manifest names every governed owner artifact; every current owner row is represented exactly once or is accompanied by a contract-backed abstain/refusal reason; the live campaign-v2 path and the legacy campaign path cannot be silently conflated.\n207\t2. **Anti-vacuity:** nonempty current fixtures exercise the path; an empty result is accepted only when the manifest and source census prove an empty governed population; removing one owner or one source makes the coverage test fail.\n208\t3. **Determinism:** input reorder, chunk-size changes, run partition changes, and repeated replay produce identical canonical references, set hash, and receipt bytes; same-byte duplicates are idempotent and conflicting duplicates refuse.\n209\t4. **Correction safety:** append correction creates a new generation and preserves the prior receipt; backdated insertion, shrink, reorder, prefix mutation, and in-place edit refuse without publishing a partial receipt.\n210\t5. **Freshness/session law:** pre-boundary rows cannot enter the forward cohort; stale or drifted source refuses; source start/end identity and hash checks are enforced.\n211\t6. **Resource truth:** benchmark receipts include measured RSS, CPU, wall, I/O, run and disk high-water; limits are enforced under a current-corpus and growth-envelope fixture; exceeding any limit refuses without truncation. The over-envelope case runs under the real service class and must end in a recorded `RESOURCE_LIMIT` refusal, never a timeout, throttle stall, or OOM kill (see “Refusal before kill”).\n212\t7. **Authority isolation:** v1 files and bytes remain unchanged; no shadow validator or second audit authority exists; trusted-context publication remains decoupled; all authority, scoring, ranking, selection, execution, trade, and publication flags remain false.\n213\t8. **Receipt integrity:** reference, audit, receipt, and HEAD schemas validate; hashes and counts reconcile; atomic publication occurs only after final validation; no failed run leaves a misleading healthy receipt.\n214\t\n215\t## Independent review findings and open gates\n216\t\n217\tThe measurement and strategy lanes agree that the stale 3,897-row and 25,000-row/48-MiB assumptions cannot be used as current capacity proof. The complete-population algorithm is bounded only by streaming/external-memory construction; the full input scan remains linear.\n218\t\n219\tA disposable benchmark over the current v1 inputs (9,641 episodes, 7,843 h60 rows, eight legacy campaigns, and canary config) produced: streaming parse/hash `0.186 s`; all-at-once JSON materialization `0.231 s`; 1,000-row external sorted-run prototype `0.663 s`, 18 runs, 30,910,185 run bytes; peak RSS `274,907,136` bytes as reported by macOS `ru_maxrss`. This is a sizing receipt for the current host and source snapshot, not a production limit: it excludes live context-store resolution, final receipt serialization, and the adjacent campaign-v2 ledgers. The implementation child must repeat the benchmark with those inputs and a declared growth envelope before freezing numeric limits.\n220\t\n221\tThe independent adversarial review added these make-or-break requirements: cross-file snapshot mutation tests; stale checkpoint and missing latest-prefix refusal; explicit inclusion/exclusion of campaign-v2 ledgers and `outcomes_session.jsonl`; correction lineage or explicit refusal; deterministic replay under shuffled maps, chunk sizes, run partitions, and interrupted writes; and resource tests beyond the historical 25k/48 MiB assumptions.\n222\t\n223\tThe charter decision is proposed for implementation planning and binds only once the commissioning seat accepts it. The bounded benchmark, adversarial review, source-manifest classification, stale-campaign refusal, correction/replay requirements, deterministic session rule, anti-vacuity tests, and refusal vocabulary are part of the implementation contract. Numeric production limits remain a mandatory implementation precondition: the implementation child must attach a benchmark receipt covering the current corpus, campaign/session growth, context-store resolution, and the declared growth envelope before code limits are accepted.\n224\t\n225\tThe implementation child is therefore separately admitted and remains held. Defaults to current working directory.","type":"string"},"glob":{"description":"Glob pattern to filter files (e.g. \"*.js\", \"*.{ts,tsx}\") - maps to rg --glob","type":"string"},"output_mode":{"description":"Output mode: \"content\" shows matching lines (supports -A/-B/-C context, -n line numbers, head_limit), \"files_with_matches\" shows file paths (supports head_limit), \"count\" shows match counts (supports head_limit). Defaults to \"files_with_matches\".","type":"string","enum":["content","files_with_matches","count"]},"-B":{"description":"Number of lines to show before each match (rg -B). Requires output_mode: \"content\", ignored otherwise.","type":"number"},"-A":{"description":"Number of lines to show after each match (rg -A). Requires output_mode: \"content\", ignored otherwise.","type":"number"},"-C":{"description":"Alias for context.","type":"number"},"context":{"description":"Number of lines to show before and after each match (rg -C). Requires output_mode: \"content\", ignored otherwise.","type":"number"},"-n":{"description":"Show line numbers in output (rg -n). Requires output_mode: \"content\", ignored otherwise. Defaults to true.","type":"boolean"},"-i":{"description":"Case insensitive search (rg -i)","type":"boolean"},"-o":{"description":"Print only the matched (non-empty) parts of each matching line, one match per output line (rg -o / --only-matching). Requires output_mode: \"content\", ignored otherwise. Defaults to false.","type":"boolean"},"type":{"description":"File type to search (rg --type). Common types: js, py, rust, go, java, etc. More efficient than include for standard file types.","type":"string"},"head_limit":{"description":"Limit output to first N lines/entries, equivalent to \"| head -N\". Works across all output modes: content (limits output lines), files_with_matches (limits file paths), count (limits count entries). Defaults to 250 when unspecified. Pass 0 for unlimited (use sparingly — large result sets waste context).","type":"number"},"offset":{"description":"Skip first N lines/entries before applying head_limit, equivalent to \"| tail -n +N | head -N\". Works across all output modes. Defaults to 0.","type":"number"},"multiline":{"description":"Enable multiline mode where . matches newlines and patterns can span lines (rg -U --multiline-dotall). Default: false.","type":"boolean"}},"required":["pattern"],"additionalProperties":false},"eager_input_streaming":true}}],"cliPrefix":"You are Claude Code, Anthropic's official CLI for Claude, running within the Claude Agent SDK.","reminderFold":false,"systemTurns":true,"toolChangeHeader":true,"inlineTools":true,"keptReminders":true,"echoWireToolInputs":true,"contextRendering":"announced"},"type":"attachment","uuid":"57c913ff-b033-41e3-8d43-e478666cd410","timestamp":"2026-10-22T05:46:56.448Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Volumes/Mastermind/agent-workspaces/claude/5600d31ffa29643a/options-intelligence-e2e-c62b5a-a093ccd8fc6d20f2","sessionId":"f77d3f70-78e4-4d1b-8664-d2f9b27233ea","version":"2.1.299","gitBranch":"claude/ssd-options-intelligence-e2e-c62b5a-a093ccd8fc6d20f2","slug":"parsed-wibbling-gem"}
{"parentUuid":"57c913ff-b033-41e3-8d43-e478666cd410","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"ac9b60721ad471e3d","attachment":{"type":"mcp_instructions_delta","addedNames":["computer-use","plugin:paper-desktop:paper"],"addedBlocks":["## computer-use\nYou have a computer-use MCP available (tools named `mcp__computer-use__*`). It lets you take screenshots of the user's desktop and control it with mouse clicks, keyboard input, and scrolling.\n\n**Pick the right tool for the app.** Each tier trades speed/precision against coverage:\n\n1. **Dedicated MCP for the app** — if the task is in an app that has its own MCP (Slack, Gmail, Calendar, Linear, etc.) and that MCP is connected, use it. API-backed tools are fast and precise.\n2. **Chrome MCP** (`mcp__claude-in-chrome__*`) — if the target is a web app and there's no dedicated MCP for it, use the browser tools. DOM-aware, much faster than clicking pixels. If the Chrome extension isn't connected, ask the user to install it rather than falling through to computer use.\n3. **Computer use** — for native desktop apps (Maps, Notes, Finder, Photos, System Settings, any third-party native app) and cross-app workflows. Computer use IS the right tool here — don't decline a native-app task just because there's no dedicated MCP for it.\n\nThis is about what's available, not error handling — if a dedicated MCP tool errors, debug or report it rather than silently retrying via a slower tier.\n\n**Look before you assert.** If the user asks about app state (what's open, what's connected, what an app can do), take a screenshot and check before answering. Don't answer from memory — the user's setup or app version may differ from what you expect. If you're about to say an app doesn't support an action, that claim should be grounded in what you just saw on screen, not general knowledge. Similarly, `list_granted_applications` or a fresh `screenshot` is cheaper than a wrong assertion about what's running.\n\n**Loading via ToolSearch — load in bulk, not one-by-one:** if computer-use tools are in the deferred list, load them ALL in a single ToolSearch call: `{ query: \"computer-use\", max_results: 30 }`. The keyword search matches the server-name substring in every tool name, so one query returns the entire toolkit. Don't use `select:` for individual tools — that's one round-trip per tool.\n\n**Access flow:** before any computer-use action you must call `request_access` with the list of applications you need. The user approves each application explicitly, and you may need to call it again mid-task if you discover you need another application. Finder is an application like any other: clicking the desktop, the Dock, or a Finder window (including Go to Folder) requires a Finder grant. The menu bar does not, as long as the app that is frontmost is one you already have access to.\n\n**Tiered apps:** some apps are granted at a restricted tier based on their category — the tier is displayed in the approval dialog and returned in the `request_access` response:\n- **Browsers** (Safari, Chrome, Firefox, Edge, Arc, etc.) → tier **\"read\"**: visible in screenshots, but clicks and typing are blocked. You can read what's already on screen. For navigation, clicking, or form-filling, use the claude-in-chrome MCP (tools named `mcp__claude-in-chrome__*`; load via ToolSearch if deferred).\n- **Terminals and IDEs** (Terminal, iTerm, VS Code, JetBrains, etc.) → tier **\"click\"**: visible and left-clickable, but typing, key presses, right-click, modifier-clicks, and drag-drop are blocked. You can click a Run button or scroll test output, but cannot type into the editor or integrated terminal, cannot right-click (the context menu has Paste), and cannot drag text onto them. For shell commands, use the Bash tool.\n- **Everything else** → tier **\"full\"**: no restrictions.\n\nThe tier is enforced by the frontmost-app check: if a tier-\"read\" app is in front, `left_click` returns an error; if a tier-\"click\" app is in front, `type` and `right_click` return errors. The error tells you what tier the app has and what to do instead. `open_application` works at any tier — bringing an app forward is a read-level operation.\n\n**Link safety — treat links in emails and messages as suspicious by default.**\n- **Never click web links with computer-use tools.** If you encounter a link in a native app (Mail, Messages, a PDF, etc.), do NOT `left_click` it. Open the URL via the claude-in-chrome MCP instead.\n- **See the full URL before following any link.** Visible link text can be misleading — hover or inspect to get the real destination.\n- **Links from emails, messages, or unknown-sender documents are suspicious by default.** If the destination URL is at all unfamiliar or looks off, ask the user for confirmation before proceeding.\n- **Inside the Chrome extension** you can click links with the extension's tools, but the suspicion check still applies — verify unfamiliar URLs with the user.\n\n**Financial actions - do not execute trades or move money.** Budgeting and accounting apps (Quicken, YNAB, QuickBooks, etc.) are granted at full tier so you can categorize transactions, generate reports, and help the user organize their finances. But never execute a trade, place an order, send money, or initiate a transfer on the user's behalf - always ask the user to perform those actions themselves.","## plugin:paper-desktop:paper\nPaper is a professional design tool for creating user interfaces. The user is working on a 2D canvas composing designs.\nThe Paper MCP server gives you tools to be a talented designer for web and mobile apps and websites. You can read designs from the user's file, see what the user is doing, and write HTML back into the design as new nodes.\n\nYou MUST load the full guide before other Paper tools: get_guide({ topic: \"paper-mcp-instructions\" }). Do this once per session; call again if a long thread may have dropped guide text.\n\n- Call get_basic_info when starting on a file to learn artboards and dimensions; use get_selection to see user focus. Omit fileId to use the file the user is looking at, or call list_resources to find another.\n- pageId defaults to the page the user is viewing; pass it to work on another page without disrupting them. You can't switch their page.\n- Typography: you MUST call get_font_family_info before your first typographic styling in a session. Prefer font families listed in get_basic_info unless the user specifies otherwise. Use px for font sizes, em for letter-spacing, px for line-height.\n- New designs: before writing HTML, generate a brief (palette, type scale, spacing, direction) unless given a design system.\n- Creating/editing: each write_html call should add roughly one visual group; prefer duplicate_nodes with update_styles and set_text_content when faster than rewriting HTML.\n- Quality: use get_screenshot to review after meaningful changes. Artboard height is a starting point — when content clips set height: \"fit-content\" via update_styles rather than guessing a fixed height.\n- Repeated rows (lists, nav): use fixed-width slots for icons and trailing actions (flexShrink: 0); gap alone won't align columns across rows.\n- When done creating or editing, you MUST call finish_working_on_nodes.\n- Never show raw node IDs to the user.\n- Export to the user's codebase: use get_jsx, get_computed_styles, get_fill_image, etc. for exact values — never read sizes or colors from screenshots."],"removedNames":[]},"type":"attachment","uuid":"951ee631-289d-4556-9771-46fc4f4f07f1","timestamp":"2026-10-22T05:46:56.451Z","rendered":[{"content":"<system-reminder>\n# MCP Server Instructions\n\nThe following MCP servers have provided instructions for how to use their tools and resources:\n\n## computer-use\nYou have a computer-use MCP available (tools named `mcp__computer-use__*`). You can read what's already on screen. For navigation, clicking, or form-filling, use the claude-in-chrome MCP (tools named `mcp__claude-in-chrome__*`; load via ToolSearch if deferred).\n- **Terminals and IDEs** (Terminal, iTerm, VS Code, JetBrains, etc.) → tier **\"click\"**: visible and left-clickable, but typing, key presses, right-click, modifier-clicks, and drag-drop are blocked. The error tells you what tier the app has and what to do instead. `open_application` works at any tier — bringing an app forward is a read-level operation.\n\n**Link safety — treat links in emails and messages as suspicious by default.**\n- **Never click web links with computer-use tools.** If you encounter a link in a native app (Mail, Messages, a PDF, etc.), do NOT `left_click` it. {"parentUuid":"d8896b6d-0d52-4431-ab9f-6054c106c067","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"103b8b66-4784-4b94-afc8-6bebca2172c3","agentId":"ac9b60721ad471e3d","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_0179t8hNXMNCcuoyZv8LpCfo","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t---\n2\tkey: W2C-V1-CONTEXT-OWNER-DECOUPLED-FROM-OPTIONS-AUDIT\n3\tquestion: >\n4\t  May W2C v1 owner replay authenticate trusted Market Memory context while the\n5\t  Options Context Audit is independently failing its frozen 4,096-reference v1\n6\t  contract, and what service boundary makes that true without masking the audit?\n7\tanswer: >\n8\t  Yes. W2C v1 consumes the trusted macro-regime profile, not the Options audit\n9\t  receipt. Production `macro-market-memory-context.service` now publishes only\n10\t  trusted context. The Options Context Audit runs as\n11\t  `macro-market-memory-options-context-audit.service` after trusted context is\n12\t  available, without Requires/Wants/PartOf, and its failure stays loud. Do not\n13\t  implement Options Audit preregistration v2 in this recovery.\n14\trationale: >\n15\t  Canonical August-13 adjudication predicted the 4,096 wall around August 20–21\n16\t  and ruled there is no lawful v1 stopgap. Production 2026-08-22 measured 3,897\n17\t  episode rows and spent the 90s CPU budget (180s wall × 50% quota) constructing\n18\t  the complete corpus, so the coupled oneshot died before W2C could reach\n19\t  w2c_reconcile_timer(). Trusted projection itself completes; the remaining CPU\n20\t  is the audit. Registration\n21\t  mmspyexpreg_e00ffc1d34b57ce3b011955a8662dae8f7e069b7f5f07417c428a5815c6dd6e3\n22\t  names trusted_profile market_memory.trusted.macro_regime_canary.v1 and does\n23\t  not name the Options audit. scripts/audit_options_market_memory_context.py\n24\t  was already the durable CLI. Splitting service ownership to match artifact\n25\t  ownership restores v1 timer arming without a second trusted store.\n26\talternatives:\n27\t  - option: Raise TimeoutStartSec or CPUQuota on the coupled context oneshot\n28\t    why_not: >\n29\t      Sol rejected a larger timeout. Even if O(N) work finished, v1 still cannot\n30\t      admit more than 4,096 references.\n31\t  - option: Widen `_MAX_REFERENCES` or the pinned receipt-store ceiling\n32\t    why_not: >\n33\t      The ceiling is byte-pinned by the sparse-selector preregistration. Lifting\n34\t      it is a preregistration act, not a buffer change.\n35\t  - option: Window, evict, or truncate episode owners\n36\t    why_not: DNR:KILL-OPTIONS-CONTEXT-AUDIT-OWNER-EVICTION.\n37\t  - option: try/except around publish_live_audit inside the context oneshot\n38\t    why_not: >\n39\t      That hides the audit behind the trusted owner and can relabel an honest\n40\t      audit failure as trusted success, or the reverse.\n41\t  - option: Manually enable --now the v1 experience timer\n42\t    why_not: >\n43\t      That bypasses owner replay. Sol forbade manufacturing an opportunity or\n44\t      starting v1/v2 experience oneshots by hand.\n45\tevidence:\n46\t  - \"VPS 2026-08-22T21:03Z: episodes.jsonl 3897 rows / 6064140 bytes; outcomes_h60.jsonl 3252 rows / 6580547 bytes\"\n47\t  - \"context oneshot 1608454 started 21:06:23Z, SIGTERM 21:09:23Z, 90.134s CPU, trusted HEAD unchanged\"\n48\t  - \"config/market_memory_spy_experience_registration.v1.json trusted_profile=market_memory.trusted.macro_regime_canary.v1\"\n49\t  - \"scripts/accrue_market_memory_spy_experience.py has no options_context / audit_options hits\"\n50\t  - \"research/options_estate/OPTIONS_CONTEXT_AUDIT_LEDGER_BOUND_ADJUDICATION_2026-08-13.md\"\n51\taffects:\n52\t  - \"WS:MARKET-MEMORY-W2C\"\n53\t  - \"WS:OPTIONS-CONTEXT-AUDIT-PREREG-V2\"\n54\t  - app/deploy/macro-market-memory-context.service\n55\t  - app/deploy/macro-market-memory-options-context-audit.service\n56\t  - scripts/project_market_memory_context.py\n57\tconfidence: high\n58\treversibility: costly\n59\tdecided_by: ceo-sol\n60\tdecided_at: 2026-08-22\n61\t---\n62\t\n63\tTrusted context and the Options Context Audit are different owners. Keep them that way.\n64\t"}]},"uuid":"3133bb16-591b-4a81-8488-b4d3d9384b85","timestamp":"2026-10-22T05:47:01.684Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"d8896b6d-0d52-4431-ab9f-6054c106c067","permissionDecision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reasonType":"mode"},"userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Volumes/Mastermind/agent-workspaces/claude/5600d31ffa29643a/options-intelligence-e2e-c62b5a-a093ccd8fc6d20f2","sessionId":"f77d3f70-78e4-4d1b-8664-d2f9b27233ea","version":"2.1.299","gitBranch":"claude/ssd-options-intelligence-e2e-c62b5a-a093ccd8fc6d20f2","slug":"parsed-wibbling-gem"}

{"parentUuid":"ad280e9c-088a-42dd-b87f-2218bbdd5a24","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"103b8b66-4784-4b94-afc8-6bebca2172c3","agentId":"ac9b60721ad471e3d","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_015mVdm4Yr8Rqd5reEZTfe3K","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t---\n2\tkey: PRODUCTION-RECORDS-ROW-BOUND-STAYS-FAIL-CLOSED-UNTIL-PREREG-V2\n3\tquestion: >\n4\t  The market-memory production-records capture refuses on the live options episode corpus\n5\t  (\"owner source artifact exceeds its row bound\": MAX_SOURCE_ROWS = 25_000 in\n6\t  engine/neuralweb/market_memory_production_records.py:76 against 29,509 episode rows, and\n7\t  the auditor's historical 48 MiB read ceiling against a 50,889,496-byte h60 outcomes\n8\t  artifact), observed on the production host on 2026-10-10 and 2026-10-11. Under the\n9\t  Chairman's 2026-10-11 \"take ownership, clear the blockers\" directive, does the seat raise\n10\t  the bound (or window the source) to make the capture run, or keep it fail-closed?\n11\tanswer: >\n12\t  Keep it fail-closed at MAX_SOURCE_ROWS = 25_000 and the pinned byte ceiling. The bound is\n13\t  re-sized only by the Options Context Audit preregistration v2 (WS:OPTIONS-CONTEXT-AUDIT-PREREG-V2),\n14\t  which must size it from LIVE measurement with stated headroom — the charter's own capacity\n15\t  refresh (research/options_estate/OPTIONS_CONTEXT_AUDIT_PREREG_V2_CHARTER_2026-09-22.md,\n16\t  \"Capacity refresh at current main (2026-10-11)\") already shows the live corpus exceeding\n17\t  the ~25k-row / 48 MiB figures the charter had been asked to size to. Sol's acceptance of\n18\t  that preregistration remains the implementation gate; the seat records the live numbers,\n19\t  does not pick a new constant, and does not window, evict, or truncate owners.\n20\trationale: >\n21\t  The row bound is a preregistered capacity claim, not a tunable: it was reviewed by the\n22\t  independent auditor against a corpus that has since roughly tripled, and the refusal is\n23\t  the honest statement that the reviewed claim no longer covers production. A constant\n24\t  chosen by this seat to \"just clear\" today's 29,509 rows would be exceeded again within\n25\t  weeks at the observed ~2,200-5,600 rows per session, would carry no review, and would be\n26\t  precisely the self-excuse the gauntlet exists to stop. Windowing or evicting owners is a\n27\t  standing kill (DNR:KILL-OPTIONS-CONTEXT-AUDIT-OWNER-EVICTION). The Chairman's directive\n28\t  clears bureaucratic blocks; it does not convert a measured data-capacity limit into one,\n29\t  and an authority override cannot cure a data-availability blocker. The lawful move is\n30\t  to put the live measurement in front of the v2 charter so the bound is sized once, with\n31\t  headroom, under review.\n32\talternatives:\n33\t  - option: Raise MAX_SOURCE_ROWS to a value above today's corpus (e.g. 50_000) and widen the byte cap to match\n34\t    why_not: >\n35\t      An unreviewed capacity claim that the observed production rate overruns again within\n36\t      weeks; it would also silently invalidate the auditor's historical read ceiling without\n37\t      a successor preregistration.\n38\t  - option: Window the source to the newest 25,000 episode rows so the capture runs\n39\t    why_not: >\n40\t      Owner eviction/truncation is a standing kill (DNR:KILL-OPTIONS-CONTEXT-AUDIT-OWNER-EVICTION)\n41\t      and would publish production records that silently omit older episodes.\n42\t  - option: Implement preregistration v2 inside this program's W8 recovery work\n43\t    why_not: >\n44\t      Out of scope and out of custody: WS:OPTIONS-CONTEXT-AUDIT-PREREG-V2 owns the charter,\n45\t      do_not_redo forbids implementing v2 inside a recovery PR, and implementation requires\n46\t      a separately keyed child after Sol accepts the preregistration.\n47\tevidence:\n48\t  - \"engine/neuralweb/market_memory_production_records.py:76 MAX_SOURCE_ROWS = 25_000 (also :369, :520, :702, :1230)\"\n49\t  - \"research/options_estate/OPTIONS_CONTEXT_AUDIT_PREREG_V2_CHARTER_2026-09-22.md §Capacity refresh at current main (2026-10-11): episodes 29,509 rows / 45,982,874 B; outcomes_h60 25,338 rows / 50,889,496 B > 48 MiB (50,331,648 B); last five sessions 5,598 / 2,161 / 2,280 / 2,319 / 2,544 rows\"\n50\t  - \"PR #7711 MERGED 2026-10-11 as d1b93722ec41 (charter edits recording the live capacity; Both files are\n42\t# byte-pinned by `research/options_estate/\n43\t# sparse_selector_preregistration_receipt_v1.json`, so editing either one is a\n44\t# preregistration act, not a buffer change.  Crossing 4,096 owner rows therefore\n45\t# requires the preregistration v2 chartered in `research/options_estate/\n46\t# OPTIONS_CONTEXT_AUDIT_LEDGER_BOUND_ADJUDICATION_2026-08-13.md`.\n47\t#\n48\t# Raising these constants buys no headroom. {"parentUuid":"d012fdf0-0f94-42bb-b10b-cf5989ea036f","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"103b8b66-4784-4b94-afc8-6bebca2172c3","agentId":"ac9b60721ad471e3d","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_0163nHz1wWBzwphcjN2Gz5zi","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Options / Market Memory context audit — the 4,096 ledger bound\n2\t\n3\t**Adjudication, 2026-08-13.** Raised by PR #5524 §\"Flagged, not acted on\" item 2, which measured a\n4\tscheduled failure in the options-intelligence estate and correctly declined to act on it inside a\n5\tCI heal. This document is the decision it asked for.\n6\t\n7\t---\n8\t\n9\t## §0 Verdict\n10\t\n11\t**A preregistration v2 is the only honest fix, and it has a hard deadline of roughly 2026-08-20.\n12\tThere is no stopgap.**\n13\t\n14\tThe obvious cheap cure — bounding the audit's input at the auditor level, which is where PR #5524\n15\tcured its sibling problem — is **closed**, not by taste but by measurement: it would manufacture\n16\tnulls inside a live preregistered evidence family (§4). The other reachable shortcut, a shadow\n17\tvalidator module that leaves the pinned file untouched, passes the pin's *check* while defeating\n18\tthe pin's *purpose*, and is rejected in §5.\n19\t\n20\tWhat ships now (§6) is therefore not a fix. It is an early-warning tripwire that converts a silent\n21\tdated outage into an actionable CI signal, plus the removal of one bound that was contradicting the\n22\trepo's own sizing of the same file. The actual fix is chartered in §7.\n23\t\n24\t---\n25\t\n26\t## §1 What fires, and when\n27\t\n28\t`data/options_signal_episode/episodes.jsonl` grows every nightly checkpoint and is never pruned.\n29\t**Four** ceilings sit at 4,096, and only the first is movable:\n30\t\n31\t| # | Bound | Location | Movable? |\n32\t|---|---|---|---|\n33\t| 1 | `_MAX_LEDGER_ROWS = 4_096` | `scripts/audit_options_market_memory_context.py:28` | **yes** — auditor is unpinned |\n34\t| 2 | `_MAX_REFERENCES = 4_096` — caps `len(references)` **and** every source artifact's `record_count` | `engine/options_market_memory_context.py:43,929,962` | **no** — byte-pinned |\n35\t| 3 | campaign-replay corpus caps on `episodes` and `h60_outcomes` | `engine/options_market_memory_context.py:677-680` | **no** — byte-pinned |\n36\t| 4 | receipt HEAD `reference_count <= 4096` | `engine/options_market_memory_receipt_store.py:403` | **no** — byte-pinned |\n37\t\n38\tBoth engine files are pinned by\n39\t`research/options_estate/sparse_selector_preregistration_receipt_v1.json` under\n40\t`selector_rule.required_truth_receipts.konseki.contract_receipts[]`, as\n41\t`reference_validator_implementation` (45,021 B / `7d3b410f…`, verified byte-identical on the tree)\n42\tand `receipt_store_validator_implementation` (21,818 B). Three\n43\t`contracts/options/options.market_memory_context_*.schema.json` files and\n44\t`config/market_memory_canary.v1.json` are pinned alongside them.\n45\t\n46\tProbed directly against the pinned validator: `record_count=4096` passes, `4097` raises\n47\t`source artifact record count is invalid`; 4,097 episodes or h60 rows fed to the campaign replay\n48\traise `campaign … corpus exceeds the bounded reference set`.\n49\t\n50\t### The countdown is ~6-7 sessions, not ~3 nights\n51\t\n52\tPR #5524 read the growth as \"~822 rows/night\". The ledger has been written exactly **twice**:\n53\t\n54\t```\n55\t  384 rows  2026-08-11  feat(options): land durable PIT episode ledgers (#5324)\n56\t1,206 rows  2026-08-13  options-pit: durable episode checkpoint 2026-08-13\n57\t```\n58\t\n59\tThe +822 was a **two-session catch-up**, not one night — bucketed by `session_date` it is\n60\t`2026-08-11: 307` and `2026-08-12: 515`. Per-session mint across the three observed sessions is\n61\t**384 / 307 / 515** (mean 402). Headroom is 2,890 rows, so the wall falls **5.6-7.2 sessions out —\n62\tabout 2026-08-20/21**.\n63\t\n64\tTreat that as a range, not a date. Mint volume is event-density-driven, not universe-driven:\n65\t2026-08-12 produced the *most* episodes (515) from the *fewest* tickers (31), one ticker alone\n66\tcontributing 168. A volatile week compresses it.\n67\t\n68\t`outcomes_h60.jsonl` is a second capped source artifact and must be tracked too, and for it the\n69\t**byte** dimension binds first: 969 rows at 1,985 B/row projects to ~8.13 MiB at 4,096 rows, so its\n70\t8 MiB `_source_artifact` cap fires just *before* its row cap. It trails episodes by roughly 9-10\n71\tsessions, so it is not the near-term deadline — but a rows-only instrument would let it walk\n72\tthrough the wall.\n73\t\n74\t### Blast radius\n75\t\n76\t`scripts/project_market_memory_context.py:170` calls `publish_live_audit` with no `try`/`except`,\n77\tafter the trusted projection has already published. The nightly `macro-market-memory-context` unit\n78\tfails every night thereafter, the options context receipt stops refreshing, and\n79\t`tests/test_market_memory_trusted.py::test_options_context_live_audit_replays_the_frozen_repository_corpus`\n80\tturns main red fleet-wide.\n81\t\n82\t---\n83\t\n84\t## §2 4,096 is an anomaly, not a considered sizing\n85\t\n86\tThe repo has already sized **this exact artifact**, deliberately and with evidence.\n87\t`engine/neuralweb/market_memory_production_records.py:55,74-76`:\n88\t\n89\t```python\n90\tSOURCE_ARTIFACT_REL = \"data/options_signal_episode/episodes.jsonl\"\n91\t...\n92\tMAX_SOURCE_BYTES = 48 * 1024 * 1024\n93\tMAX_SOURCE_DELTA_BYTES = 16 * 1024 * 1024\n94\tMAX_SOURCE_ROWS = 25_000\n95\t```\n96\t\n97\t`research/KONSEKI_CLEAN_ROOM_MARKET_MEMORY_AND_COGNITIVE_ARCHITECTURE_FOR_FABLE_2026-08-08.md:1723-1728`\n98\trecords the work behind those numbers — a hostile cold-store test at 10,384 rows / 15.8 MiB\n99\tmeasuring 340.5 MiB peak RSS and 79.3 s against the service's 2 GiB / 300 s limits — and closes\n100\twith the standing instruction:\n101\t\n102\t> \"Reaching that mark requires a separately reviewed chunked-store v2; operators must not silently\n103\t> raise v1 bounds.\"\n104\t\n105\tSo the same file is governed by two capacities that differ by **6× in rows and 6× in bytes**. The\n106\t25,000 figure is reasoned and tested; 4,096 is a generic bound that arrived in a validator and was\n107\tnever sized against this artifact. That asymmetry — not the countdown — is the real argument for\n108\tv2: the fix is to make the pinned bound agree with the sizing the house already did, once,\n109\tdeliberately, rather than to bend the audit's meaning around the anomaly.\n110\t\n111\t---\n112\t\n113\t## §3 Why the audited input must shrink, if anything shrinks\n114\t\n115\tThe receipt binds a source artifact per audited path carrying `sha256`, `bytes` and `record_count`,\n116\tand the pinned validator caps `record_count` at 4,096. It also *couples* the two\n117\t(`engine/options_market_memory_context.py:1100-1109`):\n118\t\n119\t```python\n120\tif (source_counts[\"data/options_signal_episode/episodes.jsonl\"]\n121\t        != counts[\"episode_references\"] ...):\n122\t    _fail(\"audit owner source counts differ from reference counts\")\n123\t```\n124\t\n125\t`record_count` is therefore forced equal to the episode reference count. Emitting fewer references\n126\twhile still binding the whole file is not merely dishonest, it is **arithmetically impossible**.\n127\tNor can a fifth artifact be added: `_SOURCE_PATHS` is frozen inside the pinned engine and\n128\t`build_audit_receipt` requires exactly those four in that order.\n129\t\n130\tSo if anything is to be bounded, it must be **the audited input itself**. §4 is why it cannot be.\n131\t\n132\t---\n133\t\n134\t## §4 Why windowing the audit is closed — the measurement that decided it\n135\t\n136\tThe preregistration does not only pin bytes; it binds this receipt into a decision path.\n137\t`selector_rule.required_truth_receipts.konseki`:\n138\t\n139\t```\n140\towner_binding            = campaign_v2_final_member_episode/v1\n141\tpublication_binding      = authenticated_current_private_head_contains_exact_reference/v1\n142\tmissing_or_absent_action = abstain\n143\texact_absence_reason     = exact_requested_as_of_context_absent\n144\t```\n145\t\n146\tA proposal is admissible only when the authenticated current private HEAD contains the exact\n147\treference for the candidate campaign's **final-member episode**. An episode whose reference is\n148\tabsent from the HEAD produces an abstention that is, downstream,\n149\t**indistinguishable from a genuine `exact_requested_as_of_context_absent` null**. Evicting owners\n150\tto save a buffer would be manufacturing evidence nulls in a preregistered family — the one thing\n151\tthe estate's epistemics forbid outright.\n152\t\n153\tThe question is then purely empirical: could a bounded window still cover everything the binding\n154\tcan reach? **No.** Measured on `data/options_signal_campaign/campaigns.jsonl` — campaign v2 is\n155\tlive and nightly-written by `scripts/build_options_signal_campaign.py`:\n156\t\n157\t```\n158\tcampaign v2 rows                      1,146\n159\tdistinct final-member episodes        1,146   (one per row)\n160\tfinal members by session   08-10: 369 | 08-11: 288 | 08-12: 489\n161\tmember source_row range               1 .. 1206     (ledger has 1,206 rows)\n162\t```\n163\t\n164\tCampaign v2 forms a campaign for ~95% of episodes, its bindings reach the **first row of the\n165\tledger**, and the bound set grows 1:1 with the ledger forever. There is no newest-anchored window,\n166\tno budget, and no retention rule that covers it. **The (b) branch is closed on measurement, not on\n167\tpreference.**\n168\t\n169\tFor the same reason, freezing the audit at the closed v1-era 384-row prefix is worse, not safer: it\n170\tevicts every future episode at once.\n171\t\n172\t---\n173\t\n174\t## §5 Options considered and rejected\n175\t\n176\t**Rotate or shard the episode ledger.** Closed. `market_memory_production_records.py:62,707-748`\n177\thashes the first `ACTIVATION_PREFIX_ROWS = 384` rows against a pinned digest, pins\n178\t`ACTIVATION_LAST_EPISODE_ID`, requires `record_count >= 384` and enforces append-order by\n179\t`available_at`; dropping the head raises `pre-activation owner source prefix mutated`. Campaign v2\n180\tadditionally pins `source_episode_prefix` as the exact leading byte prefix with 1-based\n181\t`source_row` indices, so sharding renumbers and invalidates every published campaign row. The path\n182\titself is `const` in two schemas.\n183\t\n184\t**A shadow v2 validator module — explicitly rejected.** Copying the engine to a new module with a\n185\tlarger bound and pointing the auditor at it would leave the pinned file untouched, so\n186\t`build_options_sparse_selector_prereg.py --check` would stay green and every pinned digest would\n187\tstill match. It must not be done. The pin exists so that the receipt is produced by *that exact\n188\treviewed validator*; satisfying the byte check with an unreviewed sibling is reinterpreting the\n189\tregistration in place, which\n190\t`research/options_estate/OPTIONS_SPARSE_SELECTOR_PREREG.md:183-185` forbids in terms:\n191\t\n192\t> \"Any candidate, decision, lifecycle, quote, or metric rule change creates a new version and a new\n193\t> forward cohort. This registration cannot be reinterpreted in place.\"\n194\t\n195\tRecording it here because it is the cheapest-looking path and a future session will find it.\n196\t\n197\t**Raise the pinned constant in place.** Not available. The pin is byte-granular, so it cannot\n198\tdistinguish a rule change from a buffer change; any edit moves `selector_rule_sha256` and trips\n199\t`origin_main_hosting_requirement` with failure action\n200\t`global_abstain_new_version_and_future_nyse_boundary_required`. There is also no v2 machinery:\n201\t`build_options_sparse_selector_prereg.py` exposes only `--repo-root` and `--check`, and the v1\n202\tschema pins every `version_fence` field — including `rule_change_policy` — as a JSON Schema\n203\t`const`.\n204\t\n205\t---\n206\t\n207\t## §6 What ships in this PR\n208\t\n209\tNot a fix — a tripwire and one honest correction. Confined to\n210\t`scripts/audit_options_market_memory_context.py` and `tests/test_market_memory_trusted.py`; no\n211\tpinned byte moves.\n212\t\n213\t1. The auditor's own unpinned read bounds go to **25,000 rows / 48 MiB**, matching §2's sizing of\n214\t   the same file, so the auditor stops being the first thing to fail and stops contradicting the\n215\t   repo's considered capacity. This buys nothing on its own — the binding ceiling becomes the\n216\t   pinned 4,096 — and the comment says so.\n217\t2. A two-tier tripwire on `episodes.jsonl` and `outcomes_h60.jsonl` independently, over **both**\n218\t   the row and the byte dimension (the byte tiers are the same fraction of the pinned 8 MiB\n219\t   ceiling that the row tiers are of 4,096, so it is one declared rule rather than two — and it is\n220\t   what keeps h60, whose byte cap binds first, from slipping past a rows-only instrument). At\n221\t   **2,600 rows** it emits a GitHub `::warning` annotation — naming the count, the headroom in rows\n222\t   and sessions, the pinned ceiling, and §7 as the owner of the fix — without failing. At **3,600\n223\t   rows** it fails.\n224\t\n225\t   The split is deliberate. A single early hard-fail would red main fleet-wide and block every\n226\t   unrelated PR for a week over a deadline that has not arrived; a single late one would leave\n227\t   barely a session of lead time, which cannot buy a preregistration v2. So the warn tier fires\n228\t   about three sessions out and the fail tier about one — the latter still landing *before* the\n229\t   nightly unit starts crashing rather than after. The test also asserts the pinned ceiling it\n230\t   guards is still 4,096, so it cannot silently stop guarding.\n231\t\n232\tThe currently-red campaign replay (`campaigns.jsonl` 8 rows vs 20 derived from the grown ledger) is\n233\t**not** touched here: PR #5524 owns that heal and is armed.\n234\t\n235\t---\n236\t\n237\t## §7 Chartered: preregistration v2\n238\t\n239\tRequired before ~2026-08-20. Scope, in the house pattern already set by campaign v1→v2 (a new\n240\tfrozen document plus a new implementation, with v1 left byte-frozen):\n241\t\n242\t* A new receipt schema, builder and receipt — v1's schema cannot express a v2 rule.\n243\t* A new forward NYSE boundary, since `selector_effective_freeze_at` and the benchmark fields are\n244\t  `const`. **This resets the forward cohort**, which is the real price of the cut and the reason it\n245\t  is an operator-visible act rather than a maintenance patch.\n246\t* Bounds sized against §2's 25,000 rows / 48 MiB rather than a larger arbitrary number, so the wall\n247\t  does not simply move. Size `outcomes_h60.jsonl` as a first-class capped source at the same time,\n248\t  and size it on **bytes**: at 1,985 B/row it reaches the 8 MiB artifact cap at ~4,225 rows, so its\n249\t  byte dimension is the binding one and a row-only v2 sizing would under-provision it.\n250\t\n251\tThe cohort reset is cheap **today** and gets more expensive every session: the selector is\n252\tcurrently `selector_active: false` with `candidate_count: 0`, all authority flags false, and the\n253\taudited `data/options_signal_episode/campaigns.jsonl` byte-frozen with no writer — so essentially\n254\tno forward evidence is being abandoned right now. That argues for cutting v2 promptly rather than\n255\tat the deadline.\n256\t\n257\t---\n258\t\n259\t## §8 Carried, not fixed here\n260\t\n261\t**237 episodes carry no H+60 outcome, and it is not a post-close-mint transient.** PR #5524 §3\n262\tflagged `osep_70fb17ad11445cca4f3f7c4b` as one episode worth confirming. Measured: **237 of 1,206**\n263\tepisodes have no row in `outcomes_h60.jsonl`, spread evenly across every session (08-10: 83,\n264\t08-11: 72, 08-12: 82 — about 20% of each).\n265\t\n266\tThe cause is structural, deliberate and already unit-tested. `derive_h60_outcome`\n267\t(`engine/options_signal_episode.py:2506`) returns a **non-persisted** `pending` with reason\n268\t`aligned_exit_crosses_session_close` whenever the cadence-aligned exit lands on or after the\n269\tsession close — which happens for any ticker whose intraday source is hourly and whose anchor falls\n270\tin the final bar. `tests/test_options_signal_episode.py` pins exactly this as\n271\t`test_coarse_aligned_exit_at_close_stays_pending_without_causal_contract`, and the code's own note\n272\texplains that persisting a coarse label would be irreproducible.\n273\t\n274\tSo the next run re-derives the identical pending result: **this is not a lane that will pick it\n275\tup**, and nothing bounds or alerts on the pending set. It belongs to the episode outcome contract,\n276\tnot to this bound — it wants either an accepted-forever disclosure with a bounded pending census,\n277\tor a finer intraday source — and should be adjudicated by the options program on its own.\n278\t"}]},"uuid":"b70f20e8-8af6-41ba-8361-4df68f594e85","timestamp":"2026-10-22T05:49:04.512Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"d012fdf0-0f94-42bb-b10b-cf5989ea036f","permissionDecision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reasonType":"mode"},"userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Volumes/Mastermind/agent-workspaces/claude/5600d31ffa29643a/options-intelligence-e2e-c62b5a-a093ccd8fc6d20f2","sessionId":"f77d3f70-78e4-4d1b-8664-d2f9b27233ea","version":"2.1.299","gitBranch":"claude/ssd-options-intelligence-e2e-c62b5a-a093ccd8fc6d20f2","slug":"parsed-wibbling-gem"}

The equality that authenticates the corpus is\n224\t    untouched -- nothing omitted, nothing extra, same rows in the same order -- and\n225\t    the frozen-ledger authenticator above still replays every committed row against\n226\t    the FULL live sources, so a v1 row that stopped being reproducible from today's\n227\t    ledger still fails there.\n228\t\n229\t    Truncation can only ever REMOVE groups: a qualifying prefix is the first one to\n230\t    cross the premium threshold, so dropping later episodes cannot promote an\n231\t    earlier prefix.  Outcomes are cut to the retained episodes because\n232\t    `derive_campaigns` refuses an outcome whose episode it cannot see.\n233\t    \"\"\"\n234\t\n235\t    frozen = options_signal_episode._as_utc(\n236\t        options_signal_episode.CAMPAIGN_RULE_FROZEN_AT, \"campaign rule frozen_at\"\n237\t    )\n238\t    closed_episodes = [\n239\t        row\n240\t        for retained = {row[\"episode_id\"] for row in closed_episodes}\n247\t    closed_h60_outcomes = [\n248\t        row for row in h60_outcomes if row.get(\"episode_id\") in retained\n249\t    ]\n250\t root=root, rows=len(episodes)),\n345\t        _artifact(h60_path, h60_body, root=root, rows=len(h60_outcomes)),\n346\t        _artifact(config_path, config_body, root=root, rows=1),\n347\t    ]\n348\t ):\n353\t        raise AuditInputError(\"deployed checkout changed during options audit\")\n354\t    audited_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat().replace(\"+00:00\", \"Z\")\n355\t    audit = BLOCKER: CH l.74 and l.192 let the seat reopen byte-pinned v1 bounds by recording a DEC.**\n- CH l.74: \"Whether any of these bounds moves is a preregistration contract decision for the commissioning seat, recorded as a `DEC:` record.\" Item 1 of that list is v1 `_MAX_REFERENCES` (CH l.70).\n- CH l.192: \"reopening one requires a material invalidator recorded by the commissioning seat.\"\n- This breaks:\n  - WS do_not_redo l.55 and l.54, which have no reopening clause.\n  - DECW l.31–34: lifting the ceiling \"is a preregistration act, not a buffer change\".\n  - DECP l.18–19: the seat \"does not pick a new constant\".\n  - ADJ l.197–203: raising the constant in place is \"Not available\"; it trips `global_abstain_new_version_and_future_nyse_boundary_required`.\n  - The charter's own CH l.24 and l.121.\n- Consequence: acceptance ratifies a second change-control path alongside SSR `version_fence.rule_change_policy = new_version_and_new_forward_cohort`.\n\n**3. MAJOR: CH l.78–80 can be read as satisfying DECP's reversal condition.**\n- CH l.78: \"The stale 25,000-row / 48 MiB figure is replaced by a stated envelope.\" CH l.80 gives explicit numbers (118,036 rows / 183,931,496 B).\n- Together these look like \"states the new bound, its measurement basis, and its headroom\" (DECP l.65–66).\n- This contradicts CH l.162 (\"Numeric limits … not chosen in this charter\") and DECP l.12–19.\n- `market_memory_production_records.py` is not in WS owns_paths (l.20–25), so no v2 artifact can resize MAX_SOURCE_ROWS. The gate is circular: DECP points to the charter, the charter (l.74) points back to a seat DEC, and DECP says the seat won't pick one.\n- Consequence: an ACCEPT could be cited to raise MAX_SOURCE_ROWS=25_000 or the auditor bound (AUD l.53–54) without any benchmark. That is exactly the alternative DECP rejected at l.33–37.\n\n**4. MAJOR: acceptance test 3 cannot be satisfied as written.**\n- The receipt must bind the run manifest (\"run digests, run count\", CH l.130) and the observed \"peak RSS, CPU seconds, wall seconds\" (CH l.131).\n- Test 3 (l.208) and l.143 nonetheless require identical receipt bytes across chunk-size changes, run-partition changes and replays.\n- Test 3 also requires \"input reorder\" to give identical output, while l.110, l.141, l.167 and test 4 (l.209) require reorder to refuse.\n- Consequence: the child must reinterpret the preregistration to pass, which is the in-place reinterpretation ADJ l.189–193 forbids. Any correct replay would also trip REPLAY_MISMATCH (l.176).\n\n**5. MAJOR: a v2 that refuses everything passes the acceptance contract.**\n- No test requires a successful receipt on the current corpus under the real service class.\n- l.81 allows any declared ceiling. l.82 makes refusal past the ceiling compliant.\n- Test 6 only requires that limits are \"enforced\". Test 2 bars only an \"empty result\", and a refusal is not one. Test 1 accepts a \"refusal reason\" for every row. Test 8 holds trivially when nothing is published.\n- The l.204 preamble makes \"acceptable\" a review of the source head only, with no live receipt required.\n- Consequence: this contradicts WS l.5–9 (\"can represent the live owner corpus … with stated headroom\"). A permanently refusing v2 could be called \"accepted\".\n\n**6. MAJOR: the fixed resource budget becomes a closed decision with no evidence it is feasible.**\n- The budget is wall ≤150 s at CPUQuota 50%, i.e. ≤75 CPU-seconds (CH l.185, l.83 \"the budget is not [the child's]\", l.195, l.192).\n- Production evidence: v1 spent 90.134 CPU-s at 3,897 rows and did not finish (DSC l.21–26). That is the per-episode stage AUD l.322–327 performs.\n- v2 must handle at least 29,517 owners (CH l.70), and the fixture is 4× that (l.80).\n- WS l.54 only bars raising limits \"as a substitute for v2\". The charter extends the prohibition to v2 itself and adds MemoryHigh/MemoryMax.\n- Consequence: acceptance may lock in a contract the corpus cannot meet, with only the seat-level reopening path from finding 2 as an exit.\n\n**7. MAJOR: the campaign path guarantees a refusal on the live corpus, and the fix has no owner.**\n- CH l.51 and l.100 require CAMPAIGN_OUTPUT_STALE / CAMPAIGN_REPLAY_MISMATCH \"until a separately accepted campaign contract resolves them\". No owner or record is named for that contract.\n- The campaign-v2 artifacts did not change between the design SHA and the refresh: same sha `0c242957…` at l.37 and l.63; same rows/bytes at l.43 and l.64. Over the same period episodes tripled.\n- The stale-campaign census (8,872 / 769) was not refreshed, and `checkpoint.json` (manifested at l.48) is missing from the refresh table (l.57–64).\n- v1 avoids the replay mismatch only by slicing its inputs at CAMPAIGN_RULE_FROZEN_AT (AUD l.202–250, l.328–338). The charter's algorithm description (l.87–94) omits this step, and its no-truncation rules (l.178, l.196) leave it unresolved for v2.\n\n**8. MAJOR: incremental generations conflict with the completeness requirement.**\n- CH l.83 allows \"each run's work bounded by the appended suffix\".\n- CH l.98 requires every governed row to be \"read, parsed, identity-checked\" on each run. l.113 requires the complete reference set.\n- The charter never says whether a HEAD produced in incremental mode contains all references.\n- Consequence: a suffix-only HEAD makes older owners resolve as `exact_requested_as_of_context_absent` under the konseki binding (ADJ l.140–151). That is owner eviction by construction, which the DNR forbids.\n\n**9. MAJOR: the charter does not bind v2 into a registration or say how it succeeds v1.**\n- SSR appears only as the pin at CH l.194. There is no new selector registration, no konseki `contract_receipts`, no `head_schema`, and no `build_options_sparse_selector_prereg.py`.\n- ADJ l.243–245 ties v2's boundary to the selector's const fields.\n- ADJ l.184–195 defines a larger-cap validator not pinned by the registration as the rejected \"shadow\".\n- SSR konseki `head_schema` is still `…receipt_head/v1`.\n- v1 stays \"independent\" (CH l.121) and keeps running (UNIT l.13; timer l.6).\n- Consequence: test 7's \"no second audit authority\" (l.198, l.212) is either violated by v2's existence or unfalsifiable.
